- **CRASH IN WATER — PHYSICS LANE (Claude, 2026-11-02, lane/claude-crash-water). Silent until the audio lane merges.**
  `StructuralDamage` no longer calls `_audio.Crash` at all. Every crash impact now goes out as a `CrashEvents`
  impact with Land or Water, and lane/codex-crash-audio's FlightAudio is what has to play it. **Merge the two lanes
  together, or crashes make no sound.** The probe and its numbers are in
  `~/Developer/agent-lanes/aero-playground/crash-water/probe.txt` (and `.csv`). Decisions for you:
  1. **Water does not break a 172 that land does.** I used the same arrival for both: 30 m/s along, 20 m/s down,
     30° nose-down. On land the 172 breaks into 7 pieces. In water it stays in one piece and floats. Each hard
     point is judged by its closing speed at first touch, and the water's added mass (von Kármán slam) takes the
     speed off before the cabin points get there: 6–7 m/s against the 8 m/s cabin break. What should break an
     airframe in water is the slam LOAD. That needs a new break criterion, and I have not invented one. The probe's
     third case forces the break-up at first water contact so you can see the pieces: nose, tail boom, both wings.
  2. **The remnant keeps the mass of what it lost.** The rule is yours: `LoseComponent` leaves mass alone, and
     `AirframeContactTests` pins it. So a cabin that has lost its engine and wings is still 1,043 kg and sinks in
     0.8 s. Its own parts would float it. I wrote mass shedding, broke that test, and took it out again. Your call
     whether the rule stands now that every lost piece is simulated as its own body.
  3. **Unmeasured openings, marked ASSUMED in `Core/Wreck/WaterPhysics.cs`.** Each one is a real dimension:
     - door-seal gap: 1 mm around two 0.96 m doors, 77 cm² in all
     - drain holes: 1/4 in, one per 0.4 m rib bay
     - cowl inlets and exit: 0.06 m² each
     - fuel vent and feed lines: 3/8 in
     - engine taken as half aluminium, half steel: 4,100 kg/m³

     Intact ditching time is set almost entirely by the door seals. Measure one 172 door gap and the number
     becomes real.
  4. **Fast ditching.** 24 m/s with the gear down: the wheels dig in (existing `WheelHydro`), it noses over to
     −38° and recovers. It stays intact apart from the prop. I expected a harder flip.
  5. **Floating bodies bob for a long time.** Wave-radiation damping is not modelled, only drag.
  - **Probe highlights** (C172 at reference load: 2 × 40 kg fuel, 86 kg pilot).
    - Pieces. Masses come from Raymer's GA weight buildup, scaled to the config's 877 kg empty mass.
      - Engine+cowl (240 kg): sinks at once and never resurfaces. Reserve gone at 0.9 s; on a 10 m bed at 7 s.
      - Each wing (156 kg, sealed tank inside): resurfaces at 1.3 s, floats, sinks at 340 s, on the bed at 351 s.
      - Tail boom (135 kg): floats, sinks at 312 s.
      - Gear legs: straight down.
    - Intact ditching (12 m/s, nose-up 8°): still afloat at 30 min, nose-down 22°, 2.9 m³ of water in, air
      escaping at 0.1 L/s.
    - Undamaged floats: 0 kg of water in 15 min.
    - Left float holed in all four compartments (rock + pump-out cap off): lists −27° at 5 min, capsizes left by
      20 min. Lessons can do this through `PracticeScenario.FloatDamage`.
  - **Unity build not run here** (a Unity chain was running), so no compile by Unity itself. I did compile the
    Bridge offline against Unity 6000.3's iOS managed DLLs (C# 9, netstandard2.1): 0 errors. No gate is marked
    approved.

- **GREAT LAKES FRONT PANEL, SECOND PASS — YOUR TWO FAULTS, AND A THIRD UNDER ONE OF THEM (Claude, 2026-11-01,
  lane/claude-gl-dials):**
  1. **The compass is a box with a window in it now.** You were right that the card hung out in the open and
     that Codex's case face was nowhere to be seen — and the cause was that I had built the thing inside out.
     The pilot sits at smaller x; the card drum's near face was 29 mm NEARER you than the face plate, so the
     drum was outside the case and the plate was buried behind it. From you outwards it now goes: the alpha
     face plate (aft-most, 2 mm proud), then the case, then the card wholly inside it. The case is **five walls
     with the aft side open** — a solid box would have hidden the very card the window exists to show — and it
     is 62 mm deep, because a 53 mm card has to fit in there, which is about how deep a real wet compass is.
     Measured off the export: the drum clears the case by 2.9 mm forward, 5.6 mm aft, 5.3 mm each side and
     9.6 mm above and below, and the face plate is unobstructed from the front eye at 25 of 25 sample points,
     so the lubber line is in view.
  2. **The chrome bezel was never broken. Its neighbours were eating it.** I measured the ring first: 384
     triangles, all 360 degrees covered, no hole — one step from telling you it was just reflection. It was the
     **tachometer's face cutting into it**. Every adjacent pair in that row of six interpenetrated, by 8 to
     16 mm; the five dark bezels had the same fault and hid it, because a dark ring cut by a dark face still
     looks like a dark ring. **My doing, last commit**: I scaled the audit's photo spacing down to the row's
     existing 290 mm width, and six real instruments do not fit in 290 mm.
  3. **So the row is spaced properly now, and that is the part worth your eye.** Sizes are real instrument
     cases — three 3-1/8 in (altimeter, ASI, tachometer) and three 2-1/4 in — and the photo's own numbers are
     what argue for it: the three small ones measure 0.71, 0.70 and 0.67 of the ASI, all within a millimetre of
     a 2-1/4 in case, and the large ones read 1.10 and 0.90 because the altimeter is nearest your oblique
     camera and the tachometer furthest. Spacing is the audit's ratios taken **as metres** — one ASI diameter
     per unit — giving a **477 mm row on a 724 mm panel**, with 12.9 mm of air at the tightest pair and 40 mm
     at the widest. The panel's toggle switch moved up beside the warning lamp, because the altimeter now
     occupies where it used to sit. **Does that row match your photograph?** It is wider than before and the
     tachometer has moved outboard.
  4. **I lit the compass card, and you should say whether you want that.** Once the card was inside a closed
     case it went dark: at 000 the render read a bare N where it had read 3 N 33 out in the open. A real
     whiskey compass carries its own lamp, so the card material is now self-lit (emissive at 0.6). It is the
     only self-lit thing on the aeroplane apart from the avionics displays. If you would rather it were lit by
     the cockpit like everything else and simply dim, say so and it comes straight back out.
  5. **Codex's band art and his face art disagree, and I cannot settle it from here — ART, NEEDS YOU.** At 000
     the card reads **N** under the lubber line and at 090 it reads **E**, so the sign is right. But only the
     letter under the line is legible: the numbers either side compress away. The audit's band contract says
     the window shows **66.8 degrees** of card, which is a FLAT strip read through a slot. The card in the
     model is a drum, and the 360-degree band has to wrap its circumference, because one degree of heading must
     be one degree of card. The window is 53.3 mm and the drum is 53.3 mm across — so the window spans **180
     degrees** of it, and everything but the middle is squeezed into the silhouette. To show 66.8 degrees the
     drum would have to be **96.8 mm across, in a case about 105 mm wide**, and the face art draws a 64 mm
     housing. So either the compass gets bigger (and Codex redraws the face), or the band is redrawn for a
     cylindrical card. Not mine to pick — it is his art and your panel.
  The reverse-sensing sign is unchanged and still verified from the seat-eye renders. **Everything from the
  first pass below still stands, including both open questions.** Re-run this round: `check_great_lakes_rig`
  59/59 — and it now measures the exported dial faces and **fails if two instruments share space**, which it
  does on the old model, all five pairs — `check_sights` PASS (8 GLBs, 808/808 rays), the sequence card still
  25 of 25 from the rear eye, `CockpitEyeCheck` PASS, `GreatLakesQuadrantShots` PASS (rear 54 mm, front 97 mm).
  Renders overwritten in `~/Developer/agent-lanes/aero-playground/gl-dials/`. No gate is marked approved.

- **DEVICE LOOK GAIN NOW DEFAULTS TO 3.5 (Claude, 2026-11-01, lane/claude-look-default) — NEEDS YOU to fly it:**
  1. **You asked for "3 to 4" after trying 1.0, 7.0 and 5.0; the default is now 3.5**, the middle of that. It was
     1.0, which is one degree of view per degree of phone — honest, but it means turning your whole body to look
     at the wingtip.
  2. **It was 1.0 because every default read the SLIDER'S FLOOR.** `MinDeviceGain` was doing two jobs: the bottom
     of the slider's travel and the value you get before you touch it. They are different questions, so the
     default is its own constant now (`CockpitLook.DefaultDeviceGain`). The floor is still 1.0 for anyone who
     wants the pure window, and the top is still 10.
  3. **Your saved setting is untouched.** The default only applies to a device that has never moved the slider —
     `PlayerPrefs.GetFloat(key, default)` — so nothing is migrated or reset. If your phone still has 5.0 on it
     from testing, it will come up at 5.0; drag the slider to 3.5 (or clear the app's data) to feel the new
     default.
  4. **The slider already spans 1–10, so 3.5 is reachable by hand**, and a test now pins that the default sits
     inside the travel and is not equal to either end.
  Not merged, not installed, not uploaded. No gate marked approved.

- **GREAT LAKES COCKPIT + ENGINE TONE (Claude, 2026-11-01, lane/claude-gl-eye) — NEEDS YOU, four things:**
  1. **The eye was 110 mm above its own windscreen.** You were right. Measured against the model: the rear
     screen's glass spans glTF y 0.167..0.377 and the eye sat at 0.487, so **no level sightline touched the glass
     at all** — looking straight ahead you cleared the frame entirely. Rear eye −0.487 → **−0.305** (182 mm
     lower), front −0.413 → **−0.293** (120 mm). x and y untouched. Each now sits where the level sightline
     passes through the MIDDLE of its own screen: rear glass −13.5° to +13.8°, front −16.5° to +16.3°, holding
     out to ±20° of azimuth. The render shows the frame around the view and the top wing's TRAILING edge
     overhead, which is what you asked for.
  2. **The throttle quadrant was never the model's fault.** `CockpitRigState.Fill` has always known how to fill
     in the throttle, the prop and mixture verniers, the key, the master and every engine gauge — and **nothing
     ever called it**. `RigInputs.Throttle` sat at 0 for the life of the aeroplane, so the lever stayed at idle
     however you flew, the prop and mixture levers sat full AFT, and every needle the rig drives from an engine
     reading pointed at zero. One call in `AirframeVisual.RigInputsNow` fixes all of it. **Worth a look on the
     Extra too** — its panel has been reading zeros for the same reason.
     The prop lever was also sharing the THROTTLE's black knob material; it is blue now, mixture stays dark red,
     throttle black. Prop and mixture are pinned full forward by a constant map, because tying mixture to the
     engine state snapped the lever aft the moment the engine stopped.
     **NOT done: the rear quadrant is still the wall-plate one, not the grey console box with short levers from
     your newest photo.** That is a regeneration of the cockpit model and I judged the functional fix the better
     use of the time. Say the word and it is a separate lane.
  3. **`check_sights` has been RED since the cockpit was restored, and it was not this lane.** It fails its
     "Place shifted" assertion on `c39451f` with the files untouched — I ran it there to be sure. The cockpit
     restoration changed the model's bounds, which moved `AirframeModels.Place`, and the spec's
     `gltf_to_body_translation_m` was left behind (the spec's own `source_transform_translation_m` already held
     the current value). I re-measured it and rebuilt the sight bars on it **and** on the corrected eye — they
     were being built on a line you could only reach by looking over the screen.
  4. **The engine tone is a feel change and the numbers are yours to move.** Every formant and low-pass dropped,
     radials hardest: Radial 7/9/14 ×0.62, flat-4 and flat-6 ×0.80, Merlin V-12 ×0.78, plus ~15% more
     exhaust-body resonance everywhere and +35% crank thump on the radials. The Radial9's voice goes from 200 Hz
     to 124 Hz. If it is now too boomy the single knob is `Formant1Hz` per profile in `EngineProfiles.cs`.
  The sequence card was moved 24 mm proud of the panel and down-left of the gauges (52° below, 30° left of the
  sightline): 17 of 25 sample points visible before, **25 of 25** now. The front panel in your photo — altimeter,
  ASI, small centre instrument, oil temp/press, oil pressure, tacho — was not compared against the model's; worth
  a look when the quadrant restyle happens.
- **DEVICE LOOK-AROUND: THE AXES WERE NEVER RESOLVED AGAINST THE WORLD (Claude, 2026-11-01, lane/claude-device-look)
  — fixed, NEEDS YOU to re-fly it:**
  1. **What you flew on 2026.1101.0549 and what was wrong.** You reported three things — turning and rolling the
     phone did the same thing, neither moved the view purely (everything came out diagonal), and tilting did
     nothing. All three are one bug. `CockpitLook.FromDevice` took `reference⁻¹ · current` and read yaw and pitch
     off that quaternion's components. **That delta is expressed in the phone's OWN axes**, so which component a
     movement landed in depended entirely on how you happened to be holding the phone when it zeroed: from any
     natural pose one physical rotation spilled into two components at once (diagonal), two different rotations
     landed on overlapping components (turn ≡ roll), and the third landed on the roll component, which was thrown
     away (pitch ignored). It was **not** a handedness bug and **not** a screen-orientation bug — no axis swap
     fixes a frame that moves with the sensor.
  2. **What it does now.** It follows where the phone is AIMED — the back of the device, its local −z — and
     resolves that against world vertical: yaw is the change in the aim's bearing about world up, pitch the change
     in its elevation above the horizon. The three come out independent by construction, and **rolling the phone
     does not move the aim at all**, so it does nothing — which is also why **portrait and landscape need no
     special case**: holding the phone the other way up IS a roll about the aim. No Unity-side change was needed;
     the bug was entirely in the Core arithmetic, and `Input.gyro.attitude`'s raw CoreMotion numbers are exactly
     what that arithmetic wants.
  3. **Please re-fly it and say whether 1× is right.** Turn the phone about the vertical → view pans left/right;
     tilt it toward or away → view pitches up/down; roll it → nothing. Each pure. RECENTRE under the look pad
     zeroes it wherever you are holding it. **One assumption I could not test from here**: that the attitude
     frame's vertical is its +z, which is what CoreMotion documents and what Unity passes through. If pitch and
     yaw come out swapped, that is the assumption — `CockpitLook.BackOf` and `ViewDeltaOf` are the only two
     places it lives, and the symptom would be a clean swap, not the mush you flew.
  4. **The old tests all passed while it was broken**, because every one of them zeroed from a tidy pose.
     `FromAnyPoseAMovementIsStillPure` zeroes from turned-37 tilted-23 rolled-61 and is the test that would have
     caught this on the bench. 62 tests in `CockpitLookTests` now, including pure yaw / pure pitch / inert roll in
     portrait, both landscapes and upside down, fed as raw CoreMotion-style attitudes.

- **MOVE THE DEVICE TO LOOK AROUND (Claude, 2026-11-01, lane/claude-device-look) — NEEDS YOU, three things:**
  1. **It is on a slider and the default is 1×, which is the one worth flying first.** Options → "Move the
     device to look around (cockpit view)", then "Look gain 1.0× (1° of device = 1.0° of view)", continuous to
     10×. 1× reads as a window: the phone is a hole in the canopy. Above about 3× it stops being a window and
     becomes a control — useful for finding the runway on base without turning your whole body, but the horizon
     no longer sits still. **Which gain feels right is yours**; the slider is there so you can land on it in the
     air rather than me guessing a number.
  2. **The look pad did NOT work while the old "goggles" option was on**, and nothing said so — `CockpitView`
     returned out of the frame before the hat ran, so switching the option on silently removed the on-screen
     arrows. They compose now: `CockpitLook` keeps the hat's offset and the device's apart and clamps only the
     sum, so a snap over the shoulder plus a lean of the phone add up, and letting go of the pad no longer
     snatches the view back from where you are holding the device. RECENTRE sits under the pad and zeroes both
     against however you are holding it at that moment.
  3. **There is no tilt steering in the game, so nothing double-counts — today.** `Input.gyro`,
     `Input.acceleration` and the InputSystem sensors appear in `CockpitView.cs` and nowhere else; tilting the
     phone moves the view and only the view. If tilt steering is ever added it must take its OWN reference and
     its own zero, because feeding one attitude to both would make looking out of the left window roll the
     aeroplane left. That is written at the point where the gyro is switched on, where whoever adds it will be
     standing.
  Limits unchanged and still enforced on the SUM: 160° of yaw, 75° up, 60° down. At 10× that is reached after
  16° of device, and turning back brings the view straight back rather than unwinding stored offset, which is
  asserted. Not VR: this is one eye, not stereo — the seat eye points are the basis for that when it comes.

- **THE EXTRA'S PROPELLER TURNS (Claude, 2026-11-01, lane/claude-prop-spin) — NEEDS YOU, two things, and one
  correction to what I told you earlier:**
  1. **Fixed, and it was only the Extra.** A model with its own rig skips the hinge carve — correctly, carving a
     rigged model tears the draughtsman's parts — but the carve is ALSO where a model's propeller is handed to
     the turning hub. So `SpinProps` turned a hub with nothing on it while the blades stood still, and the
     procedural blades that would have turned were hidden with the rest of the procedural shell.
     `AirframeBuilder.BindModelProp` now re-parents the model's `Prop` and `Spinner` nodes onto the hub, centred
     on the spinner, with the blades on their own bone so the blur disc can still take over above ~350 rpm.
     **I told you earlier that the Pitts and the Great Lakes were static too. They are not.** Neither of their
     rigs binds, so both go down the carve path and both propellers were already turning — the carve skins the
     blades to the hub's bones, which is why the node's transform never moves and why the first version of this
     check reported four false failures. `PropSpinCheck` knows the difference now.
  2. **The Great Lakes' rig is dead and says nothing about it.** `docs/models/great-lakes-own-rig.json` names 58
     nodes and **all 58 are missing from `great-lakes-like-own.glb`** — it was written for a cockpit that never
     shipped in that GLB. `AirframeRig.Bind` returns null when NOTHING binds and logs only when something does,
     so a rig that matches nothing is completely silent. The aeroplane flies (the carve picks up the surfaces and
     the propeller), but none of its instruments move and nobody would know. Two separate calls for you: whether
     the Great Lakes should get its cockpit GLB rebuilt to match the rig, and whether `Bind` should shout when it
     binds zero of N.
  3. **The 172's and the J-3's propellers really are static**, for a different reason: the carve cannot separate
     the propeller from those downloaded meshes, and `AirframeVisual` deliberately handles that by keeping the
     model's own blades and suppressing the blur disc. `PropSpinCheck` reports it rather than failing, because
     fixing it means re-cutting someone else's mesh. Your call whether that is worth a lane.
- **SIDESLIP → NOSE DOWN, built (Claude, 2026-11-01, lane/claude-sideslip-camber) — NEEDS YOU, three things:**
  1. **These are feel numbers, not measurements, and they are yours to move.** You described the mechanism
     (low pressure over the turtledeck against higher pressure under the fuselage; low pressure under the cowling
     against higher pressure above — both even in beta, so either direction pitches down) and said the Pitts is
     "similar but less pronounced". Nothing here is from a wind tunnel. At 50 m/s and 15° of sideslip:
     | | turtledeck | cowl | total | as pitch accel |
     |---|---|---|---|---|
     | Extra 300 | −540 N·m | −112 N·m | **−652 N·m** | −0.593 rad/s² |
     | Pitts S-2B | −173 N·m | −37 N·m | **−210 N·m** | −0.187 rad/s² |
     The Pitts comes out at **0.32 of the Extra's**. Most of that is geometry — smaller side area, shorter arms
     — and the rest is a lower coefficient (0.6/0.35 against 1.0/0.6) because the S-2B's turtledeck is rounder.
     If "less pronounced" should be nearer a half than a third, raise `pitts-s2b-like.fuselage.crossflow`'s two
     `*CamberCl` and nothing else needs to change.
  2. **Half of the Extra's was already written down and had never once acted.** `cowlCamberCl` was `0.0` and
     `AeroModel` gates on `> 0.0`, so the forward half of the mechanism was in the config, looked present in a
     diff, and did nothing. It is 0.6 now. The Pitts had no camber keys at all — absent, not reduced. Every test
     in `SideslipCamberTests` isolates the camber by recomputing with both coefficients zeroed, which is the only
     way to tell "authored" from "authored and reaching the aeroplane".
  3. **The Great Lakes still has no camber authored and I left it that way.** You named the Extra and the Pitts;
     the Great Lakes is the third type with a deep turtledeck and a cowled engine and it has none of this. It is a
     one-line change once you say what it should feel like, and `ATypeWithNoCamberAuthoredStillHasNone` currently
     pins it at zero, so it will fail loudly the moment someone adds it without deciding.
  Not retuned: this moves the Extra's and the Pitts' pitching moment in a slip, which is spin and tumble
  behaviour. The spin tests pass, but they are coarse; the aeroplane is the real check.

- **SEAT EYES / COCKPIT CAMERA (Claude, 2026-10-31, lane/claude-eyepoints) — NEEDS YOU, six things:**
  1. **Does the Great Lakes rear eye look right?** You asked about "3 cm below the top line". Measured against
     the real GLB, not the procedural shell: the REAR eye (-1.960, 0, -0.487) is **3.5 cm below the top of the
     Headrest**, and the headrest is the ONLY structure above it at that station — which is where a headrest
     belongs. The FRONT eye (-1.193, 0, -0.413) has `Wing_Upper` 43.5 cm overhead and the windscreen ahead,
     which is what a front cockpit under a top wing should see. Both read right to me; the rear one is yours to
     confirm, because 3.5 cm of headrest is the difference between sensing it behind you and peering under it.
  2. **The weight-and-balance decision is no longer theoretical — it was costing real mass.** Three aeroplanes
     were flying far under their authored weight because the two schemas collide on the key name
     `loading.emptyMassKg`: great-lakes-like 816 -> 557.919 kg (-258, 32% light), j3-cub-like 553 -> 346.998
     (-206, 37%), glider-swift-s1-like 330 -> 280 (-50, 15%). `SessionSettings.ApplyLoadTo` runs on EVERY spawn,
     so that was the flying mass in the GAME; the .NET suite builds an Aircraft without calling ApplyLoad and so
     never saw it. I set the key to 0 so it derives from `mass.massKg` again and kept the authored figure as
     `_legacyEmptyMassKg`. **The two systems still coexist** (`src/FlyingGame.Core/Loading/WeightAndBalance.cs`
     and `AircraftLoad.cs`; `loading.stations` and `loading.seats`) and only one should survive. Not my call.
  3. **The new seats carry placeholder mass stations and conventional placards.** For the 2-33, Great Lakes,
     Pitts and Extra no W&B seat station is authored anywhere, so `station` equals the EYE — a placeholder, not
     a weight-and-balance arm. `maxKg` is a conventional 113.4 kg (250 lb) occupant placard, and the J-3's own
     authored 136.08 kg (300 lb); none of these is read from a POH. `referenceKg` follows the 172's convention
     — the PRIMARY seat carries the 86 kg pilot that `mass.massKg` is assumed already to include — so the
     reference loading still reproduces the authored mass, CG and inertia exactly and **nothing moves at
     spawn**. **Whether each airframe's authored massKg really includes a pilot is the open question.** I had
     these at maxKg 0 first, so nobody could load a seat while the decision is open; that tripped the older
     rule in AircraftLoadTests that every station must carry a placard, and the older rule is the better one.
  4. **The Extra's propeller does not turn**, and it is the only type with its own authored rig. The hinge carve
     is skipped for a rigged model — correctly, carving one would tear its geometry — but the propeller binding
     lives inside the part that is skipped, so `SpinProps` runs every frame over an empty `_props`. Trap: the
     rig DOES have an entry whose param is `prop`, but `Prop_R` is the blue rpm vernier KNOB on the rear panel;
     the blades are separate `Prop` and `Spinner` nodes. A fix that matches nodes CONTAINING "prop" spins the
     knob. Not fixed here: it is an AirframeVisual change and this is a camera lane.
  5. **The sideslip-to-pitch coupling you described is half-built.** On the Extra the turtle-deck term is live
     (`sectionCamberCl` 1.0, area 2.8, x -1.9) but the cowl term is authored with a ZERO coefficient
     (`cowlCamberCl` 0.0, area 1.2, x +1.5), and `AeroModel.cs:1082` gates on `> 0.0`, so the cowl never acts.
     The Pitts and the Great Lakes have no camber keys at all — the effect is absent, not reduced. Picking those
     coefficients moves the spin and tumble behaviour, so it wants its own lane and its own run against the spin
     tests.
  6. **check_iac_assets.py is RED and I did not force it green** (inherited from the sights merge). The Extra
     carries two sequence-card quads from two lanes: the IAC holder's card and the cockpit panel's own slot.
     From the rear eye `Cockpit_Interior` blocks 81 of 99 sample points on the card and the duplicate slot
     blocks 72. Which quad should survive, and at what depth, is a modelling decision.

  mm, all fourteen seats); the Great Lakes rear eye is no longer inside the fuselage (808/808 rays clear); and

- **START (Claude, 2026-10-30): lane/claude-island-nav off 1fe347d for Phase 2 IFR + weather.** Gate confirmed read-only in `~/Developer/flying-game-worktrees/codex-island-world/AGENT-LOG.md` line 753, copied here verbatim and not moved or edited:

- **WHY IT OPENED AND STILL WOULD NOT FLY: the view and the controls were one bug, and it was neither (Claude, 2026-10-31). FIXED.** Codex's `e2f4fd6` made the world affordable (70.4 -> 0.78 us a sample) and Ty installed it at 05:40 PDT: it plays. It then showed two faults — the camera far away with the aeroplane a tiny speck below it, and controls that did nothing. **One cause, and it is not the camera rig and not the input binding.** `WorldTerrain.Airports` reads the island's eight airports, but `WorldTerrain.AirportStrips` was still the four-airport world's template — one 2,400 m runway along +x at every field, with a crosswind runway along +y — and `ChooseRunway` was applying it to the island's airport centres. Every start therefore sat 1,570 m off the field on a heading of 000, on ground that has nothing to do with the pad:

  | field | template start | island ground there | field elevation | out by |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | PHDH | (4330, 4990) | 789.1 m | 4.3 m | **785 m underground** |
  | PATK | (3420, -6340) | 906.8 m | 111.3 m | **795 m underground** |
  | 3U2 | (-2330, 1820) | 1363.7 m | 1511.8 m | 148 m in the air |
  | KMYL | (1210, -880) | 394.6 m | 1531.3 m | **1,137 m in the air** |
  | KMEV | (-3300, -3020) | 1402.1 m | 1439.9 m | 38 m in the air |
  | PANC / KPGA / KSNA | — | — | — | 30 m, 24 m up; KSNA 7 m under |

  PHDH is `DefaultAirportIndex`, so the FLY button's own default start was 785 m inside a hillside. **An aeroplane under the ground reaches `Math.Sign(NaN)` in `LandingGear.Compute` (LandingGear.cs:428) and throws `ArithmeticException` on its FIRST step** — reproduced in a probe, stack trace and all. `FlightSimDriver.Update` has no catch, so `ApplyStateToTransform()` never runs: the airframe never moves and nothing the pilot does reaches the sim. Meanwhile `ChaseCamera.KeepAboveSurface` — which by owner rule may never go below the ground — lifts the camera to 790 m every frame and re-aims it down at an aeroplane 785 m below. **That is the wrong view and the dead controls, both of them, from the one spawn.** Ty's `AERO_SELFTEST=perf` run was clean because the perf test flies and never sat on that runway.
  **The fix, consuming Codex's geometry and changing none of it.** New `src/FlyingGame.Core/IslandRunways.cs` (mine) serves the island's own runways as the `RunwayEnd` the spawn, tow, approach and practice paths already speak, read straight from `IslandField` — real centre, real true heading, real length, real surface, real slope — and chooses an end by headwind for arbitrary true headings, the crosswind choice being a runway on a genuinely different axis (KMEV 17/35 vs 13/31) rather than the other end of the same one (KSNA 2L and 2R are parallel and are not a crosswind choice). `WorldTerrain.ChooseRunway` delegates to it when a world is installed and keeps the old template as `ChooseTemplateRunway` for the widget and the flat-ground tests — that is the only edit to `WorldTerrain.cs`, three lines and a comment, and no airport, runway, pad or elevation was touched. `FlightSimDriver.Spawn` now takes its pad elevation from the ground at the start point rather than a field-wide figure (3U2 slopes 0.5 %, Cabin Creek 3 %), and in free flight takes its airborne start from `IslandSites.Starts` — the world's own authored position, true heading and altitude — instead of deriving one, because a derived start has no way to know the ground 600 m off KMEV's runway is 1,100 m lower. Events keep their own starts.
  **What says it works.** `TheRunwayChoiceAgreesWithTheStartTheWorldAuthored`: in calm air the end my chooser picks matches Codex's authored `OnTheRunway` start **to 0.1 m and 0.1 degrees at all eight airports** — the world and the sim now agree about where a flight begins, which is the real proof that this reads the island rather than guessing at it. `EveryAirportStartsOnItsOwnRunway` holds the start on a runway of that field, on ground within 1 m of the strip's own plane, across 8 wind directions x headwind/crosswind x calm/breeze. `NoStartPutsAnAeroplaneInsideTheGround` holds the 172 and the Cub on their wheels (CG 0.2-6 m above the pad) and both the on-final and airborne starts clear. `TheControlsMoveTheAeroplaneAtEveryStart` is the direct regression for the dead controls: full throttle for 15 s rolls the 172 **220-260 m and reaches 28-30 m/s at every one of the eight airports**, and asserts no NaN. New `AERO_SELFTEST=starts` walks all eight airports x six start modes on the device and reports, per start, the height above ground, where the camera ended up relative to the aeroplane, Unity errors thrown, and how far the aeroplane moves when the controls are pushed.
- **THE SAME BUG AGAIN, IN THE WATER (Claude, 2026-10-31). FIXED.** Ty flew the first fixed build and the J-3 on
  floats launched frozen: 12 ft, 10 kt, alpha 180, beta +90, nothing on the pads moving it. Those are not flight
  values, they are the spawn, frozen — and every one of them is arithmetic. `WorldTerrain.Lakes` is the
  four-airport world's water the way `AirportStrips` was its land: one lake 3 km south-west of every field at the
  field's elevation less 2 m. On the island, PHDH's is at 2.3 m where the ground is **450.1 m and there is no
  water at all**; PATK's is 798 m under; KMYL's 254 m under; and the flying boats' HARBOR stands **1,529 m up a
  mountain**. Only PANC and KSNA land on open sea, by accident. 12 ft is PHDH's phantom lake surface (2.3 m) plus
  the Cub's float keel (1.55) less its resting draft (0.25); 10 kt is the 5 m/s session wind; beta 90 is that
  wind from the east on an aeroplane heading north; alpha 180 is what is left when there is no forward airspeed
  at all. The state never advanced because the first step went to NaN, 448 m inside a hill.
  **Fixed** by asking the island for its own water (`IslandRunways.Water`): the nearest piece at roughly the
  field's own height that is long enough for the hull. It now picks what a pilot would — PANC gets **Lake Hood**
  1.3 km away, KMYL **Payette Lake** 1.2 km (McCall really is on it), KPGA **Lake Powell**, PATK the AP8 float
  pond, and the coastal fields the sea 2 km out. Nearest-by-distance alone was not enough: it sent PHDH's Cub,
  which lives at 4 m by the sea, to an alpine tarn 2,030 m up a mountain because it was 3 km closer, so the
  choice weights every metre of climb as ten metres of travel. Flying boats need 2.2 km and no authored lake has
  it, so the H-4 goes to sea, found by walking outward from the field until there is a clear run — not by me
  inventing a harbour, because the coast is Codex's. `EverySeaplaneStartsOnWaterDeepEnoughToFloat` holds it for
  the J-3, PA-18 and Beaver on floats and the H-4, at all eight fields: water present, bed below it, long enough,
  and the resting spawn above the bed.
  **The device sweep had been running the 172 alone, which is exactly how this got past me.** It now sweeps a
  floatplane too (96 starts, not 48), and it survives a start that throws instead of dying on the first one — the
  thing built to find bad starts was being stopped by the first bad start.
- **WHAT THE PITTS "FREEZE" ACTUALLY IS: low-altitude flight costs 13 fps, and the water query is why (Claude,
  2026-10-31). NOT FIXED — the expensive part is Codex's, handed off below.** Ty: "I was not underwater when it
  froze I was flying around at ~3,000 ft." He was right and my first answer was wrong. Reproduced on the iPad
  with `AERO_SELFTEST=cruise`, a Pitts holding altitude for three minutes:
  `t=0s 61 fps agl 412` ... `t=30s **13 fps** agl 10` ... `t=36s **13 fps** agl 19` ... `t=42s 17 fps agl 1`.
  **At 412 m above the ground it runs at 61 fps; at 10-20 m above the ground it runs at 13.** Nothing else
  changed — same aeroplane, same flight, no errors, heap flat at 33-36 MB. And note what "3,000 ft" means on this
  island: the inland fields are at 1,500 m (4,900 ft), so 3,000 ft on the altimeter over the plateau is BELOW the
  terrain tops, in a valley, at low AGL. His cruise was a low-level cruise whether or not he meant it to be.
  Measured per query, on this machine: `GroundHeightAt` **3.6-4.2 us** (Codex's index, working), but
  `FloatHydro.WaterSurfaceAt` **7.5 us over the sea and 14.4 us at KSNA**, and `WheelGroundHeightAt` 4.4-9.6 us.
  The contact solver asks for all of them for every contact point at every RK4 stage: 15 points x 4 stages x
  200 Hz is 12,000 of each per second of flight, and the altitude gate I added only closes above 60 m AGL, which
  is exactly where the frame rate is fine anyway. Below it, the water query alone is 90-170 ms of CPU per second.
- **[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: `IslandTerrain.WaterAt` never got the treatment `HeightKm` did, and it is now the
  most expensive thing in low-level flight** (src/FlyingGame.Core/IslandTerrain.cs:75). Every call walks the whole
  world again: `World["water"]` by dictionary lookup and an `EllipseRadius` per body, then every entry of `Fields`
  by `Contains`, then — on dry land above sea level, which is most of the island — `IslandWater.StreamAt`, which
  walks **every canyon with a full `Path()` polyline distance** and then every field a second time. Measured
  **7.5 us over the sea, 14.4 us at KSNA**, against **3.6-4.2 us** for the indexed `GroundHeightAt` beside it.
  **Needs:** the same bounded-cost treatment as the height field — a spatial index so a sample only visits the
  water bodies, fields and canyons whose footprints contain it, with an early-out for the common case of dry land
  nowhere near a stream. Target the same order as the height query. For scale: at 14 us a Pitts flying 10-20 m
  above the ground runs at **13 fps on an iPad mini**; the same flight 400 m up runs at 61. I have not touched any
  world file or the water geometry.
- **Mine to weigh after that, and NOT done:** the contact solver asks the ground and the water for every point at
  every one of RK4's four stages, though the aeroplane moves less than 0.2 m between them. A per-step memo would
  cut both query counts fourfold for a bounded error, and is worth doing whatever Codex's index costs — but it
  changes forces within a step, so it wants the same 180-state proof the altitude gate got, not a quick patch.
- **`Screen.sleepTimeout` was never set, anywhere in the project (Claude, 2026-10-31). FIXED.** Every flight ran on
  the device's own auto-lock timer, so hands-off at altitude — which is most of a cross-country — the screen locks,
  the app suspends, and what the pilot sees is a frozen picture with dead controls that comes back when he taps.
  Now `SleepTimeout.NeverSleep` in `SceneBootstrap`. **I offered this to Ty as the explanation of his freeze and
  then withdrew it**: the 60-second perf run completed fine on the same device, which a 30-second auto-lock would
  have cut off, so the dropped console sessions were the link, not the screen. It is the right setting regardless
  and may still be what his iPhone was doing; the measured 13 fps above is the explanation I stand behind.
- **STILL OPEN, mine: 3.5 seconds of locked frame on every FLY.** `[Freeze] SLOW FRAME 3,508 ms` every single
  launch, while the forest streamer builds its first ~100 chunks with nothing budgeting that first batch. Felt on
  every flight, every time.
- **STILL OPEN, mine: an aeroplane at rest on water runs at 8-35 fps** where one at rest on a runway runs at 60.
  Same cause as the low-level cost above: every point is in contact, and every point pays the water query.
- **(SUPERSEDED by the entry above, kept for the ruling-out it records) The Pitts "freeze" (Ty, 2026-10-31).** Not the same bug and
  not the simulation. Ruled out by measurement, not by reading: the island height field has **no non-finite value
  anywhere** (40,401 samples on a 200 m grid across the full 20 km ring); a Pitts driven into terrain at 90 ft/s
  at eight places — KSNA, mid-island, Drake, KMYL, 3U2's slope, Powell canyon, the sea and the PHDH hill —
  survives all eight with no NaN and no exception; and **sixty seconds of island flight is clean in the sim** for
  both the Pitts and the 172, worst single sim-second 288 ms of CPU on this machine. Ty's screenshot shows
  ordinary flight values at the moment it stopped (54 kt, alpha 61.4, 90 ft/s down, mid-spin, descending toward
  terrain), which is consistent with the frame stopping rather than the aeroplane. So it is above the sim.
  New `FreezeWatch` (always on, one float comparison a frame) prints a line for every frame over 300 ms and a
  heartbeat every 5 s, carrying where the aeroplane is, the camera's height above ground, the forest streamer's
  live/queued/worst, the bubble count and the managed heap — so a freeze that cannot be reproduced on demand
  leaves evidence instead of silence. `AERO_SELFTEST=perf` now takes `AERO_TYPE` and `AERO_START`, so the
  measurement can be pointed at the Pitts in the box instead of only the 172 off a runway.
- **Test baseline on this lane: 102 failures at the merge commit, 102 with all of this work — my changes added
  none.** They are the four-airport world's geography (Alaska 10, RegionalAtmosphere 9, NavProcedure 8,
  AlpineRange 7, WaterfallTunnel 6, Seaside 5, Navigation 5, HighDesert 5, PlateauCopy 5, FrankChurch 4 ...),

- **[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: the S3 thermal start and its lift centre are 1,200 m apart.** `starts[id=S3].centre` is (E 4.5, N -4.6) km while `lift_terrain[id=S3].centre` is (E 5.7, N -4.6) km. Codex's decision of 2026-10-31 is to keep the lift centre and move the start; both live in the world data, so the move is Codex's. Nothing is broken by it today — the start is 457 m above good ground — it simply does not begin in the lift it is named for, so a thermal start there is a glide. `TheOnlyThermalStartAwayFromItsLiftIsS3` holds the line meanwhile: it passes with this one mismatch, passes once the start moves, and fails if a second one appears.
- **THE APP OPENS AND DOES NOT PLAY — root cause confirmed and quantified, and it is NOT fully fixable in my lane (Claude, 2026-10-30).** It is neither a crash nor a hang nor a config failure. Build 2026.1030.2201 on Ty's iPad mini, launched through `devicectl --console` with `AERO_SELFTEST=perf`, which drives the menu's own FLY path: `StartMenu:Fly()` ran, the aeroplane spawned, the simulation ran and kept running. It ran at **5 fps, with 220–235 ms of CPU main-thread time per frame against 4–5 ms of GPU**. No exception, no NaN, no watchdog kill, GPU idle. The aeroplane flies; it flies far too slowly to fly.
  **Why, measured on this machine, one aeroplane and no AI traffic:**
  - `AirframeContact.Compute` cost **1,488 us per call** — 99 us for each of the 172's 15 contact points — against **37 us for the entire aerodynamic pass**. Contact, not aero, not graphics.
  - One island height query costs **~70 us**, because `IslandTerrain.Evaluate` walks the whole world description per sample: both coast polygons, regional overrides, crests, peaks, water, glaciers, canyons (a nested loop over every path segment, twice), lesson terrain and the outer islands. Its exact-coordinate memo cache cannot help a moving aeroplane, whose every query is a fresh coordinate.
  - The contact loop asks for one per point, and RK4 evaluates forces **four times per step at 200 Hz**: about **12,000 height queries per second of flight**, ~1.2 s of CPU per second, for one aircraft.
  - The 15 AI aircraft `CombatController.Start()` creates are **not** the cause: they only step within `ActiveRangeM` (3 km) of the zone. I nearly fixed that and checked first.
  **What I fixed (mine, measured):** `AirframeContact.Compute` now tests the footprint first — the ground under the CG and at four points on the airframe's own radius, five samples instead of fifteen per stage — and skips the per-point loop only when the lowest part of the aeroplane is more than 60 m above the highest of them. Airborne cost fell from **1,643 to ~655 ms** per second of flight (2.6x). `SkippingContactAtAltitudeNeverSkipsARealContact` holds the gated forces identical to the exact per-point test across 180 states — flat field, Drake's vertical north cliff, a canyon, the sea, upright, banked and inverted — because a shortcut that misses a contact is a worse bug than a slow frame.
  **Why that is not enough, and what needs you.** In cruise it is still **1.5x slower than real time on a Mac Studio for a single aeroplane**, and on the runway — where every one of your eight start airports begins — it is **1.9x**, because near the ground every point must be tested and each test costs 70 us. No arrangement of Claude-owned code makes contact physics affordable while one height query costs 70 us. **This is a blocking Codex dependency, handed off below.** Per the prompt I have not started the IFR or weather work, which is gated behind the startup repair.
- **[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: island height-field query cost makes the simulation unplayable, at `IslandTerrain.Evaluate`/`HeightKm` (src/FlyingGame.Core/IslandTerrain.cs:58-130).** Measured on a Mac Studio: **70.4 us per fresh query** (20,000 random samples inside the ring), 16.9 us when ~90 % of them repeat and hit the memo cache. Every sample evaluates the entire world description, including `Shore()` against both coast polygons, every crest and glacier via polyline distance, and every canyon via a nested double loop over its path segments. The physics needs thousands of fresh queries a second near the ground and cannot batch or repeat them, so the exact-coordinate `[ThreadStatic]` memo at line 60 never helps a moving aeroplane. **Needs:** a bounded-cost height query — a baked height raster with bilinear sampling, or a spatial index so a sample only visits the features whose footprints contain it. Target on the order of 1 us. For scale: at 70 us the 2026-10-30 build ran at 5 fps on an iPad mini (220 ms CPU, 4 ms GPU); the same world at 1 us would leave contact physics at about 6 ms per second of flight. I have not touched any world file.
- **[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF is not needed for this one, it is mine, logging it so it is not lost:** `configs/atmosphere/regional-atmosphere.json` anchors two air masses to `airport:Bench` and `airport:Mesa`, which the island world does not have — `WorldTerrain.Airports` now comes from `IslandTerrain.World["airports"]` (PHDH, PANC, PATK, KSNA, KMYL, 3U2, KMEV, KPGA). The device log shows `[Atmosphere] regional config not loaded (unknown airport anchor 'airport:Bench') — uniform air`, so the whole regional weather model is silently off. Re-authoring it is Phase 2 weather work on this lane.
- **UNITY LIBRARY CACHES ARE CAPPED NOW, AND THE QUEST LANES DO NOT SHARE ONE (Claude, 2026-11-02,
  lane/claude-deploy-cache-cap).** `~/Library/Application Support/FlyingGame/unity-Library-<lane>` was 164 GB
  (12 caches; the four Android/Quest ones 24.7 GB each) with the disk 97% full. `deploy.sh` now touches its own
  lane's cache and runs the new `tools/deploy/prune-library-caches.sh` before every build: at most 8 caches
  (`FG_CACHE_KEEP`) and 80 GB (`FG_CACHE_MAX_GB`), oldest first. Never the current lane's cache, and never one that
  a running Unity, xcodebuild, bee_backend, gradle, java or Unity dotnet process uses (argv, cwd, the worktree in
  `-projectPath`, or an open file via lsof). That check runs again right before each removal. When over the size
  cap it first **trims** build output (PlayerDataCache, Bee, il2cpp_cache, BuildPlayerData), which every deploy
  `rm -rf`s anyway and which is 20 of an Android cache's 24.7 GB. Only after that does it delete whole caches.
  `--dry-run` prints the plan. A pruned lane's missing cache is recreated empty by deploy.sh, which means a full
  re-import, not a broken symlink.
  Dry run on 2026-11-02 at 10:59 PST, from claude-island-nav, with a Unity/xcodebuild chain running in claude-crash-test (kept: in use):
  delete inertia-b-deploy, inertia, codex-vr-quest, vr-stereo (47.8 GB); trim claude-vr-cockpit, vr-quest (20.4
  GB each), codex-crash-audio, claude-crash-water, codex-extra-wing-b (3.6 GB each). That would free 99.3 GB and
  leave 8 caches at 64.7 GB. With `FG_CACHE_TRIM=0` it would instead have to delete 8 caches, vr-quest among them.
  **Decision for you: a shared Android cache.** Not done, because it is unsafe here. Unity locks a project with
  `Temp/UnityLockfile` in the *project* folder, not in Library, so two worktrees whose Library is the same folder
  are not stopped from opening it at once. The four VR lanes run batch compiles in parallel (serialised only by a
  `pgrep` check, which can race). One lane would then load the other lane's ScriptAssemblies and import results.
  Run strictly one at a time, sharing would be correct (Unity re-hashes and re-imports what differs). It would also
  cost a full re-hash on every lane switch. The trim gets most of the space back without that risk: an idle
  Android cache drops to ~4 GB. If you still want sharing, it needs a real lock around every Unity launch, not
  only around deploys.

- **The rigs mirror is no longer committed on this lane, and merging it needs `lane/claude-config-mirror` first (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs/` was gitignored-but-tracked here, the same trap that let great-lakes-like ship with no runtime config: `git add` on a new rig mirror does nothing, only `git add -f` works, and nothing notices. The mirror is now untracked and gitignored; `ConfigSync` on this lane still writes it at editor load, so the lane works standing alone. **Ty: merge order is `config-mirror`, then `flow-plates`, then `extra-cockpit`, then `sgs233-rig`.** `ConfigSync.cs` WILL conflict when this lane merges after `config-mirror` — both rewrote the file from the same base. The resolution is fixed: **take `config-mirror`'s version** (its `Mirrors` table already declares the rigs mirror and derives the destination name from each rig table's own `model` field), **drop this lane's bespoke rigs block, and re-add `AirframeRig.Forget()` where a changed rig is written** — that call clears an editor-side cache and `config-mirror`'s generic sync cannot make it, because `AirframeRig` does not exist on its base. **This lane is already pushed**, so the remote still carries the committed mirror until you push this fix.
- **The Extra's cockpit wants a flight on a device (Claude, 2026-10-30).** All 60 rig nodes are bound and measured correct in the editor (worst pivot error 0.1 mm), but nobody has sat in it. Worth checking: (a) the REAR seat is the default view and the eye height feels right — OPTIONS > Display has a seat picker; (b) the sequence card — tap OPTIONS > Display > Card > Choose PDF, pick a sequence off the device or iCloud Drive, step pages, and Default to put the IAC card back; **this is the only part that cannot be tested anywhere but a device**, because the picker and the rasteriser are iOS's (UIDocumentPickerViewController + PDFKit); (c) the canopy — OPTIONS has Shut/Open, it refuses to open above 60 kt and shuts itself if you accelerate with it open; (d) bail out and watch the real canopy leave, and confirm the aeroplane stays canopy-less until the flight is reset.
- **Four gauges on the Extra's panel have no sim behind them (Claude, 2026-10-30), and you should decide whether that is acceptable.** The sim has no oil, no cylinder heads, no manifold and no fuel quantity, so manifold pressure, oil pressure, oil temperature and CHT are a PRESENTATION model built from throttle, rpm and ambient pressure (`CockpitRigState.FillEngineGauges`, marked (P)), tuned so the needles sit inside the dial markings the model is drawn with. The FUEL gauge reads full always, because an engine that cannot run dry should not have a needle falling toward E. Nothing here reaches the flight model. The alternative is a real engine-systems model in Sim, which is a bigger piece of work and a physics change this lane was told not to make.
- **Codex: the Extra's ASI prose and its map disagree (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `docs/models/ea300-own-RIG.md` says the ASI needle "rests at 0 below 40", but the map in `ea300-own-rig.json` is 0 kt -> 0 deg, 40 -> 25, 260 -> 335, so the needle lifts off the peg smoothly from a standstill. The DATA is the better behaviour (that is what a real ASI does) and it is what the game now draws; the prose is the loose part. One line in the doc, no rebuild.
- **Merge order for the four ready lanes (Claude, 2026-10-30): `config-mirror` → `flow-plates` → `extra-cockpit` → `sgs233-rig`.** `config-mirror` first: it removes the aircraft trap and adds the guard and tests the rest assume. `flow-plates` second: independent (`AirframeFlow` only), and it takes the failure baseline from 4 to 3, leaving just the three Pitts ground-handling tests awaiting your feel-test. Then the two rig lanes, each of which **will conflict on `ConfigSync.cs`** — both rewrote it from the same base. The resolution is fixed: take `config-mirror`'s version, drop the lane's bespoke rigs block, and re-add `AirframeRig.Forget()` where a changed rig is written (it clears an editor cache; the generic sync cannot call it because `AirframeRig` does not exist on this base). `sgs233-rig` is last because it branched at `ed2cc7ca`, not `1b67dd5`, so it needs bringing forward.
- **StreamingAssets is generated now; three pushed/unmerged lanes still carry a hand-committed mirror (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `lane/claude-config-mirror` makes `configs/**` + `docs/CREDITS.md` the only copies in the repo and has the build write the player's. **Ty: two things.** (a) `lane/claude-extra-cockpit` is **already pushed** and adds a fifth mirror class, `StreamingAssets/rigs/` (`docs/models/*-rig.json`, with a filename mapping), committed by hand. Merging it reintroduces the trap unless whoever merges adds a rigs row to ConfigSync's `Mirrors` table (it needs a name-mapping field the struct does not have yet), gitignores `rigs/` + `rigs.meta`, and `git rm -r --cached`es the copy. `lane/claude-sgs233-rig` carries a second rigs file. `StreamingAssetsTests.NothingGeneratedIsCommitted` fails until that is done, by design. (b) `lane/claude-glacier-ski`, `claude-glacier-visuals`, `claude-j3-edo1320`, `claude-regional-atmosphere` and `claude-soft-wings` each carry a mirror copy that will conflict trivially — take the deletion side.
- **The load sheet and the envelope plot are in, and three more types have certificated CG limits (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `lane/claude-engine-systems`. **Landing page → AIRCRAFT → Load sheet**: a slider per seat, hold and tank, Full/Half/Default fuel, and the type's own CG envelope plotted with the loaded point on it. Loading it aft and flying it feels different, because the config is what the physics reads. **Ty: please thumb it on a device** — the 172 and the J-3 Cub are the two that can be loaded, and the J-3 is the interesting one (which seat the pilot sits in is worth 8 %MAC). Two things to know: (a) **the plot shows where the aeroplane is, and I believe that is right** — your charts-never-show-position rule is about navigation, and a weight-and-balance envelope carries no geography and is unreadable without the point, so I have shown it and written the reasoning into the file rather than leave it to be "fixed" either way later. Say the word and it comes out. (b) **The Aero Widget already had a weight-and-balance control** (protocol 8, your 2026-10-09 ask) and I did not duplicate it: it stays the teaching instrument — two abstract sliders that will happily go outside the limits to show why they exist — while the load sheet is the real thing. But it was carrying invented limits (`empty ≈ 62 % of gross`, `CG ± 9 % MAC`) and a comment saying the configs have no POH envelope; it now uses the certificated envelope where the type has one, and its limits move with weight as a real envelope does.
- **Weight and balance is built, and the published data says two things about the fleet (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `lane/claude-engine-systems`. You can now load an aeroplane — seats, baggage, cargo, fuel in each tank — and the mass, CG in all three axes and inertia go into the aircraft's own config, so all 93 CG read sites fly the loaded aeroplane with no change. Done for `c172-like` (Cessna 172N POH + TCDS 3A12) and `j3-cub-like` (TCDS A-691); the rule for adding a type, and everything below, is in `docs/WEIGHT-AND-BALANCE.md`. Moving the 172's front occupants into the back seats moves the CG 9.3 %MAC aft and takes 9.4 %MAC off its measured static margin — the handling follows the load, measured rather than asserted. **Ty: three things need you.** (a) **Every type is simulated at max gross and most sit a little nose-heavy of where a real one balances** — the 172 at 27 %MAC against about 32 %MAC for a typical gross load, so roughly 5 %MAC more stable than the real thing. Closing that means moving the tuned CG, which retunes pitch stability on calibrated aeroplanes; your call, not a quiet fix. (b) **The CG positions look authored by convention**: seven types land within 0.1 % of each other at exactly 27.0 %MAC. Sensible as a default, not the same as knowing where the aeroplane balances. (c) **`pa28-archer-like` is blocked on TCDS 2A13** for the Archer's MAC and LEMAC station — the POH gives the arms and the envelope but defines its datum off the wing leading edge, and anchoring there puts the published aft limit at 18 %MAC and the config's CG behind it. The 172 hangs together under the same treatment, so it is the Archer's anchor that is wrong, and I have not guessed it.
- **The StreamingAssets trap has a second mouth: `git add` on a tracked-but-ignored file exits 1 AND stages it anyway (Claude, 2026-10-30).** Reproducible on this lane: `git add unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/aircraft/j3-cub-like.json` prints "The following paths are ignored... use -f", returns exit code **1**, and leaves the file staged. So a script that trusts the exit code would believe nothing happened, and a mirror edit can ride into a commit without anyone typing `-f`. It nearly got me. There is a second edge too: `GlideTableTests.EveryAircraftPassesTheNewAirplaneProtocol` fails if you run the suite after editing a config and passes once you build, because `ConfigSync` regenerates the mirror during the build — so its result depends on build order rather than on the code. `lane/claude-config-mirror` is the cure for both; until it merges, an aircraft JSON change on any other lane leaves a modified-but-uncommittable mirror file behind.
- **Engine reliability, engine systems and weight-and-balance are one piece of work, and the first part is built (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `lane/claude-engine-systems`. Searching for engine state returned NOTHING — no oil, MP, CHT, mixture or fuel anywhere — which is why the Extra's four gauges read dead. And CG is static JSON read in **93 places across 41 files**, which is what W&B has to make live. Order agreed with Ty: W&B, then engine systems, then the failure symptoms. The randomizer is done (`29fe6bb`): measured MTBF 3700.6 h against 3700 over 74 million simulated hours, failures spread over 12 causes without the rate drifting, and `m` clamped to [1, 1000] so an engine is never better than the fleet. **Ty: two things need you.** (a) The **carburettor icing envelope must come from FAA SAIB CE-09-35 itself** — my fetch timed out, and a version circulating online is provably wrong (it plots dew point above ambient temperature). If you have the SAIB or the PHAK figure to hand, that table is otherwise guesswork. (b) The **failure-cause weights are an allocation, not a measurement**: the NTSB record counts accidents, and most of its fuel share is exhaustion and mismanagement, which the random pool deliberately excludes. A service-difficulty or warranty-claim distribution would be the right source and I could not find one.
- **Ty's answers of 2026-10-30, recorded so they outlive the chat (Claude).** Ten open questions went to Ty and all ten came back:
  - **The Extra's four unbacked gauges -> build real engine systems in Sim.** Oil, cylinder heads, manifold and a fuel quantity that burns down. A physics change, its own lane, not started. It retires the presentation model in `CockpitRigState.FillEngineGauges`.
  - **EA300 GPL -> closed. Ty: "I don't consider this to be based on his drawing."** His determination on the facts; the item is off this list. But `docs/models/ea300-own-PROVENANCE.md` still says the geometry was measured from the FlightGear mesh and **needs rewriting to match** -- not yet done.
  - **J-3 63 in chord -> wait for Codex's corrected model**, then change aero and model in one commit so the drawn and the flown wing never disagree.
  - **StreamingAssets `git add -f` trap -> Claude's call**, which is: untrack all four mirrors, have `BuildScript` call `ConfigSync.Sync()` itself so the build PRODUCES its data instead of inheriting it from an `[InitializeOnLoad]` side-effect, fail the build when a config has no mirror, and make ConfigSync DELETE stale mirrors -- it only ever copies today, so a renamed config leaves its old self in the player for good. Not started; it is a build change on the trunk.
  - **Pitts ground handling -> Ty has not flown it; the three tests stay red.** Nothing forced green.
  - **Crevasses -> fatal, but only where they are drawn big enough to see and avoid from the pattern.** Needs Codex to draw them at the size the physics uses.
  - **Extra span 7.43 vs 7.99 m -> find out which variant we are building first** (EA300/L is 8.00 m, SC is 7.50 m) before touching the config.
  - **Great Lakes stall -> back-solve CLmax from the published 57 mph**, and write down that the derivation ran backwards.
  - **Flow plates -> fix properly, a surface starts at the skin.** DONE, see the Log entry below.
  - **Lanes -> push the finished one, clear the dead worktrees.** Done as far as is safe; see the Log.
- **The Extra's wing has one bad measurement station, and it costs roll rate (Claude, 2026-10-30).** In `configs/aircraft/extra-300-like.json` at y = +-2.523 m, THREE values jump and then snap back, in both `wing` and `wing-aileron`: z goes to +0.049 where every neighbour is -0.0735 (12.2 cm up), the aileron hinge x to -0.562 against an interpolated -0.681 (22 cm forward), and the aileron chord to 0.493 against an interpolated 0.338 (+46 %). The wing's own chord there (0.928) sits exactly on the taper, so the station was measured fine and the z/aileron probe caught something standing proud -- the hinge bracket or a balance horn. That strip is ~0.60 m of span, so the inflated chord adds about **+9 % aileron area per side**: the Extra's roll rate is likely several percent high from one probe. **Claude's suggestion:** interpolate the station from its neighbours and MEASURE the roll-rate change; ask Codex to re-measure y = +-2.523 on the model first, because if the model really is kinked there the config is right and the fix reverses; and do it in the span-variant lane so the wing is touched once, not twice. Untouched so far.
- **26 of 28 branches had never been pushed (Claude, 2026-10-30).** Only `lane/claude-extra-cockpit` and `lane/claude-j3-edo1320` exist on GitHub; everything else lives on one Mac and nowhere else. **Ty: say the word and I push the finished lanes.** I did not put eleven branches on a public repo unasked.
- **`main` is a week behind and is not where work lands (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `main` is at `860dd96` (10-23); `split-surfaces` is at `1b67dd5` (10-28) and is the real integration branch. NOTHING is merged into `main`, so "is it merged yet" asked against `main` is always no -- which is how the worktree cleanup first came back empty. **Ty: either fast-forward `main` to `split-surfaces`, or say plainly that `split-surfaces` is the trunk.**
- **The paraglider and the hang glider fly, but they have no MODEL (Claude, 2026-10-30).** Both are in the fleet picker and both hit their published polars in the sim, but neither has a GLB, so `AirframeVisual` falls through to its default "high-wing strut-braced tandem trainer": they fly right and look completely wrong. **Codex:** a paraglider is a 25 m^2 canopy on ~6.2 m of line with a seated pilot in a harness, and a hang glider is a 14.4 m^2 swept flex wing with a prone pilot under a control frame, hanging 1.09 m below. `docs/SOFT-WINGS.md` has every dimension.
- **No ram-air canopy yet (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `Parachute.cs` is a properly modelled ROUND emergency canopy (28 gores, D0 8.5 m, Knacke fill, opening shock, 6.2 m/s sink) and it was left alone -- it is the right canopy for a bailout from an aerobatic aeroplane. But "flying" a round is 0.48:1 and a heading change. A sport ram-air is a WING: ~3:1, 10 m/s forward, 3.5-5 m/s down, brakes that trade speed for sink, and a flare that can put the descent near zero. That is a second canopy type, and it is the one piece of the four-item brief that is **not built**.
- **The hang glider's published stall does not close, by 7 % (Claude, 2026-10-30).** 30 km/h at 110 kg over 14.4 m^2 demands CL 1.78, which no sail section gives. Read as a minimum CONTROLLABLE speed rather than a CL break, CLmax 1.55 (the top of the credible range for a cambered double-surface sail) puts the break at 32 km/h. **Ty: if that feels too fast, the lever is the all-up weight or the area, not the section** -- 1.78 is not available. A manufacturer polar for any specific glider would settle this and the glide and sink figures too, which are currently CLASS figures: Wills Wing publishes Vne, sizes and weight ranges but no polar. That is the weakest number in `docs/SOFT-WINGS.md`.
- **Launch is untouched for both soft wings (Claude, 2026-10-30).** They spawn in the air at trim speed. Foot-launching off a slope -- which is how both types actually start -- and a tow for the hang glider are separate work, and the slope launch wants terrain that Codex's island world will provide.
- **The Great Lakes ships with NO runtime config on the trunk, right now (Claude, 2026-10-30).** `GlideTableTests.EveryAircraftPassesTheNewAirplaneProtocol` fails on clean `split-surfaces` at `1b67dd5` with: *great-lakes-like: StreamingAssets copy missing or different*. This is the `git add -f` trap from the StreamingAssets item, live on the trunk and already caught by a test. `lane/claude-glacier-visuals` has the one-line fix (`beea9ff`) and it never reached `split-surfaces`.
- **Aerobatic sights — Great Lakes visibility decision needed (Codex, 2026-10-31, lane/codex-sights).** Exact requested body eye (-1.970, 0, -0.264) is occluded by current opaque fuselage under the measured current importer transform: 0/606 sight visibility rays pass. The historical eyepoint transform differs by about 223 mm vertically. Eye coordinates/status and cockpit geometry are preserved. Choose whether to retain this as an unresolved proposal or authorize an eye/placement/cockpit correction. Full-scene evidence, separate frame-isolation diagnostics and exact transforms: `docs/pending/sights-renders/`, `docs/models/sights-PROVENANCE.md`. No gate approved.
- **Aerobatic sights / lowered Pitts card need external Blender + Unity review (Codex, 2026-10-31).** Eight livery GLBs updated through the pure-Python immutable-baseline path; full Blender commands are in `docs/models/sights-PROVENANCE.md`. Numerically all bars are within 0.000004 degrees of vertical/45 and centres within 0.000001 degrees. Pitts/Extra visibility and all types’ static/full-aileron-travel clearance bounds pass. Pitts card moved down 50 mm, with no instrument moves needed. Operator: rebuild all liveries in Blender and review/import in Unity; `src/` and `configs/` untouched. Great Lakes visibility remains flagged separately; no gate approved.
- **A green test suite is not a build, and it cost us a day's build (Claude, 2026-10-28).** `BiplaneCellule` used `{ get; private init; }`. The .NET 8 suite compiled it and reported 776 passing; Unity's compiler has no `System.Runtime.CompilerServices.IsExternalInit`, so the same file was 30 x CS0518 and the iOS player would not build **at all** from 14:41 to 16:22. Fixed in `141be0b`. **Anyone finishing a change that touches `src/` must run `tools/deploy/deploy.sh` and see `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, not just the test suite** -- they are two compilers and two runtimes, and only one of them was being asked.
- **The Great Lakes needs a look, and three things on it are not sourced (Claude, 2026-10-28).** It is in the fleet picker and flies; see the Log. (a) Its **stall is 10 % low** — Vso 51 mph against the manufacturer's 57 — which is a lift limit, so no drag change moves it; closing it needs a lower cellule CLmax and the wing section is not known for this type. (b) Its **inertias are estimates** scaled off the Pitts and the Extra; there is no mass breakdown. (c) Its **CG height** (0.25 m below the thrust line) is derived — a TCDS gives no vertical CG. Also: the GLB is exported with the gear EXTENDED, so until the oleo rig is animated the drawn wheels hang about 0.23 m below where the physics has the aeroplane standing. **Ty: fly it and say whether it feels right**, and if you have a section or a weight-and-balance sheet for the type, those are the two numbers that would close it.
- **Pitts ground handling after the rebuild (Claude, 2026-10-28): three tests are red and I do not think they should be forced green.** With the wing quarter-chords corrected, the drawn main wheels sit 0.39 m ahead of the CG (they were 0.23 m), and the vertical tail lost 12 % of its area, so the Pitts is far more ground-loop prone: the scripted pilot swings 91–97° off the runway on the landing roll (`ThreePointAndWheelLandingsAreWorkable` x2, `PittsNeedsQuickFeetTheExtraForgivesRelaxedOnes`). I tried the tailwheel steering spring (15 → 25/40/60) and its cornering stiffness (2200 → 3200/4500); neither moves it, because the cause is geometric, not a tuning value. Two facts say the aeroplane is right and the benchmark is the stale part: TCDS A8SO says the S-2B moved its "wings and landing gear forward 6 inches" against the S-2A, and the scripted pilot is already faster than human on the rudder (no rate limit on its command; the surface slews at 229°/s, neutral to full in 0.13 s, and `Kr = 6` saturates the pedal at a 9.5°/s yaw-rate error — it runs out of authority, not speed). **Ty: fly a few landings and tell me whether it feels like an S-2B or like a bug.** If it is right, `QuickFeet` wants raising for this type; if it feels wrong, the drawn main-wheel station is the thing to question (Codex).
- **Pitts CG height (Claude, 2026-10-28).** `mass.cg` z is 0.20 m below the datum, derived from what the ground handling needs, because a TCDS gives no vertical CG. The station (x) is now sourced from TCDS A8SO. A mass breakdown per the new-airplane protocol's step 1c would settle the height.
- **Flat-spin smoke check is red (Claude, 2026-10-28), and it is not the flight model.** `AirframeFlowTests.SmokeGoesRoundTheAirframeInTheFlatSpin` reports a marker inside the airframe. Cause: one plate covers a whole tailplane including the part inside the fuselage, so near the root "off the plate" and "outside the body" are mutually unsatisfiable and `KeepOut`'s two pushes swap the marker back and forth. It surfaced because the Extra's tail is now at the arm its drawing gives it, which roots the stabiliser further aft in a thinner part of the body. I had a partial fix (skip a plate where it is buried in the body); it cleared the tail root and then failed the same way at the wing root, so I reverted it rather than leave an unverified change to shared flow code inside this diff. The fix belongs in how AirframeFlow builds its plates — a surface should start at the body skin.
- **The menu overhaul wants a look on a device (Claude, 2026-10-28).** OPTIONS is eight chapters and the landing page five. It ran clean through `AERO_SELFTEST=menu` at all four screen shapes, but nobody has thumbed it yet. Check in particular that a chapter never needs its scrollbar on an iPhone in portrait, and that the "world paused" note reads right.

- **EA300 GPL — decision needed before App Store release (Claude's review, 2026-10-27; see the Log entry below).** Our own EA300 is now the in-game Extra. Claude's assessment: treat the airframe shell (v2–v12) as a likely derivative of Patrizio Melis's GPL FlightGear EA300, and treat GPL content as not safe to ship through the App Store. **Ty: pick one before App Store release:** (a) ask Patrizio Melis (github.com/cobe571) for written permission to use the measured shape in a closed, commercial App Store game (a draft request is in the log entry); or (b) put the v12 cockpit, engine, inlets, prop and paint onto the clean-room v1 shell (638c05a). This does not block TestFlight or internal builds.
- **Pitts / Extra ground handling (Claude, 2026-10-28): please feel-test on a device.** The gear now matches the models and both types were retuned (see the Log entry). Things to try:
  - Pitts: taxi, a full-rudder turn at walking pace, and three-point and wheel landings. It is meant to need quick feet: relaxed rudder on the rollout loops it, active rudder holds it.
  - Both: full brakes at a fast taxi (12 kt). This still noses over (Pitts flips, Extra strikes its prop), while half brakes, or full brakes at a walk, do not. That is realistic, but if it feels harsh on the touch pad, the knob is the global brake capacity in `LandingGear.Compute` (the 2026-09-13 0.675 tune), not these configs.
- **Left over from matching the models (Claude, 2026-10-28): physics left unchanged, decide if wanted.**
  - Pitts: the drawn thrust line is 0.135 m above the sim's CG (it was 0.33 m), so it still appears to pivot a little low. Closing that means moving `mass.cg` z or `propulsion.thrustLineZ`, which is a flight-model change.
  - Extra: the config's tail surfaces sit about 0.5 m ahead of the drawn tail. The config span is 7.43 m against the model's 7.99 m, so the model is drawn at 93 %. Takeoff lift-off is 74 kt, against about 65 kt published for the Extra 330LT.
  - Pitts takeoff roll is 135 m, against 170 m (557 ft) published; acceleration comes from the shared propulsion model.
- **Livery picker (Claude, 2026-10-28): please look at it on a device.** The LIVERY row is now one drop-down for both types: four Pitts schemes (Checkered, Factory, N31DG, N1191) and three Extra (Unmarked, Talk Flying, N300UY). It compiles and the swap is verified in the editor, but nobody has seen it on an iPhone or iPad yet. `AERO_SELFTEST=menu` saves `selftest-menu-<shape>-livery.png` (Pitts selected) for each of the four screen shapes. Also check that the unmarked defaults are what you want a new player to see first.
- **Extra unmarked label (Claude, 2026-10-28):** I named it "Unmarked", not the HANDOFF's "Extra". The AIRCRAFT field right above it already says Extra 300, so "Extra" would repeat it. Easy to rename in `LiveryChoice.Options` and `Livery.UnmarkedExtra` if you prefer something else.
