- [codex → Claude] aircraft-config · codex/own-j3-cub · J-3 v15 COCKPIT RIG (Ty: "make the J-3 fully animatable so Aero can drive it") · for configs/aircraft/j3-cub-like.json and j3-cub-floats-like.json · NOT yet reviewed by Ty:
  - Both GLBs (wheels and floats) were rebuilt. The full spec is in docs/models/j3-cub-own-RIG.md. Every value below is also stored in the node's glTF extras.
  - **Convention**
    - The frame is glTF: +x aft, +y up, +z port, with the origin at the config datum (unchanged).
    - Each node rotates or translates about its own origin. Rest rotation is identity.
    - Extras:
      - rig_param: shared names mean the parts are linked;
      - rig_axis_gltf (rotation) or rig_move_axis_gltf (translation);
      - rig_map_param / rig_map_deg (or rig_map_m): a piecewise-linear table, offset from the modelled pose, clamped at the ends;
      - rig_map_extrapolate=1 on the altimeter hands; rig_map_wrap=360 on the compass;
      - rig_param_model; rig_detents on the mag switch.
    - Apply it as q = AxisAngle(axis, deg) * q_rest.
    - Reference implementation: tools/own_models/j3_fourview.py with J3_POSE.
  - **Hierarchy:** Cockpit > Instruments (needles, Compass_Card, Primer_Plunger) and Controls (Stick_*_Roll > Stick_*, Pedal_*, levers); MagSwitch_Knob sits directly under Cockpit.
  - **Parameters:**
    - aileron, elevator, rudder, trim: −1..1 (+ = roll right / stick aft / right pedal / nose-up);
    - throttle 0..1; fuel_valve (1 = ON); carb_heat 0..1; magneto 0..3 (OFF/L/R/BOTH); primer 0..1;
    - engine_rpm, airspeed_mph, altitude_ft, oil_temp_f, oil_psi, heading_deg.
  - **Linkage, to be driven together:**
    - the front and rear sticks;
    - all four pedals with the Rudder (±30), TailWheel_Steer (±30, wheels) and WaterRudder_L (±25, floats);
    - Trim_Crank with Stab (trim +1 = LE down 4°, −1 = LE up 2.5°; Elevator_L/R are children of Stab);
    - both throttles;
    - CarbHeat_Lever with CarbHeat_Knob_Rear.
  - The surface nodes (Aileron_L/R ±18, Elevator_L/R +34/−29, Rudder, TailWheel_Steer, WaterRudder_L, WaterRudder_Blade_L water_rudder_up, doors, FuelGauge_Wire, Prop) keep their v7 extras (rig_deg_pos/neg). They are listed in the RIG doc.
  - **Cockpit nodes** (wheels GLB values; the floats GLB is identical):
      - Needle_Tach (parent Instruments): engine_rpm rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.58, 0.233, 0.164], 0→0, 500→5, 3500→275 deg
      - Needle_Airspeed (parent Instruments): airspeed_mph rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.58, 0.233, 0.075], 0→0, 40→12, 140→312, 150→327 deg
      - Needle_Alt_100ft (parent Instruments): altitude_ft rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.5792, 0.233, -0.095], 0→0, 1000→360 deg rig_map_extrapolate=1
      - Needle_Alt_1000ft (parent Instruments): altitude_ft rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.58, 0.233, -0.095], 0→0, 10000→360 deg rig_map_extrapolate=1
      - Needle_OilTemp (parent Instruments): oil_temp_f rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.58, 0.2378, -0.181], 100→0, 250→120 deg
      - Needle_OilPress (parent Instruments): oil_psi rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.58, 0.2089, -0.181], 0→0, 80→120 deg
      - Compass_Card (parent Instruments): heading_deg rotate, axis [0.0, 1.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.579, 0.233, -0.011], 0→0, 360→360 deg rig_map_wrap=360.0
      - Throttle_Lever_Front (parent Controls): throttle rotate, axis [-0.0, -0.0, 1.0], pivot [-0.482, 0.065, 0.3056], 0→0, 1→41.3322 deg
      - Throttle_Lever_Rear (parent Controls): throttle rotate, axis [-0.0, -0.0, 1.0], pivot [0.388, 0.065, 0.3075], 0→0, 1→41.3322 deg
      - FuelValve_Lever (parent Controls): fuel_valve rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [-0.303, 0.035, 0.3074], 0→90, 1→0 deg
      - Trim_Crank (parent Controls): trim rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [-0.252, -0.087, 0.3066], -1→-450, 0→0, 1→450 deg
      - Stab (parent None): trim rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [4.373, 0.485, 0.0], -1→2.5, 0→0, 1→-4 deg
      - CarbHeat_Lever (parent Controls): carb_heat rotate, axis [0.0, -0.0, -1.0], pivot [-0.358, -0.064, -0.307], 0→0, 1→35 deg
      - CarbHeat_Knob_Rear (parent Controls): carb_heat translate, axis [0.99546, -0.09488, 0.00701], pivot [0.333, -0.127, -0.2903], 0→0, 1→0.035 m
      - MagSwitch_Knob (parent Cockpit): magneto rotate, axis [-0.08196, -0.99664, -0.0], pivot [-0.259, 0.6609, 0.215], 0→0, 1→-37, 2→-73, 3→-115 deg rig_detents=['OFF', 'L', 'R', 'BOTH']
      - Primer_Plunger (parent Instruments): primer translate, axis [1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.57, 0.164, -0.24], 0→0, 1→0.05 m
      - Stick_Front_Roll (parent Controls): aileron rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [-0.267, -0.4576, -0.0], -1→-20, 0→0, 1→20 deg
      - Stick_Front (parent Stick_Front_Roll): elevator rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [-0.267, -0.4576, -0.0], -1→-15, 0→0, 1→20 deg
      - Stick_Rear_Roll (parent Controls): aileron rotate, axis [-1.0, 0.0, -0.0], pivot [0.483, -0.4077, 0.0], -1→-20, 0→0, 1→20 deg
      - Stick_Rear (parent Stick_Rear_Roll): elevator rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [0.483, -0.4077, 0.0], -1→-15, 0→0, 1→20 deg
      - Pedal_Front_L (parent Controls): rudder rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [-0.697, -0.395, 0.13], -1→-18, 0→0, 1→18 deg
      - Pedal_Front_R (parent Controls): rudder rotate, axis [-0.0, -0.0, 1.0], pivot [-0.697, -0.395, -0.13], -1→-18, 0→0, 1→18 deg
      - Pedal_Rear_L (parent Controls): rudder rotate, axis [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], pivot [0.033, -0.395, 0.13], -1→-18, 0→0, 1→18 deg
      - Pedal_Rear_R (parent Controls): rudder rotate, axis [-0.0, -0.0, 1.0], pivot [0.033, -0.395, -0.13], -1→-18, 0→0, 1→18 deg
  - **Judgement calls, for Ty to confirm:**
    - stick travel ±20° roll and +20/−15° pitch at the grip;
    - pedals ±18°;
    - trim crank ±450° (1.25 turns each way);
    - carb heat lever 35° and rear knob 35 mm;
    - primer 50 mm;
    - the fuel valve is a 90° quarter-turn.
  - Gauge dials are as painted: ASI 40–140 mph over 300°, tach 500–3500 over 270°, oil 120° arcs.
  - The heel brakes are static. There is no brake rig.
  - The painted needles were removed from the panel atlas, so Aero must drive the needle nodes. At the rest pose they read engine off, 0 ft and heading 000.
  - Contact sheet on the agent box: /workspace/j3-cub-v14/rig/.

## Log
### 2026-11-02 — Grok Bot · lane/claude-cockpit-look · cockpit view "all sky" on the iPhone with the 8BitDo
Report (Ty, iPhone, build `2026.1102.1603`, pad connected): select the cockpit view and all he sees is sky.
- **Ruled out:** no gamepad axis reaches the look. `GamepadInput` feeds flight only; `CockpitView` read only the
  legacy hat (`JoystickInput.HatButton0` = 28, and `ButtonHeld` is `b < 20` on iOS, so inert there) and the
  on-screen look buttons.
- **Cause:** his prefs (read off the phone, `Library/Preferences`): `view.deviceLook = 1`, `view.deviceGain = 4.92`.
  `ApplySettings()` at `Start` enables the gyro and immediately `Recentre`s against `Input.gyro.attitude`, which is
  EXACTLY the identity (phone flat, screen up) until CoreMotion's first sample. With the reference set,
  `FromDevice` never re-zeroed. A phone held up in landscape = back swung from straight down to level = +90° pitch,
  ×4.9, clamped at `MaxPitchUpDeg` 75°: parked looking up. Nothing recentred on selecting the cockpit view.
- **Fix:** `CockpitLook.IsUnsampled(q)` (bit-exact identity, zero-length or non-finite). `Recentre` with one leaves
  the zero empty so the first real sample becomes straight ahead; `FromDevice` ignores one. `ChaseCamera.SetView`
  → `CockpitView.OnEnterCockpitView()` recentres (hat and device) every time the cockpit view is entered (Y).
  Gamepad: R3 recentres; L3 held + d-pad = look (pan held, snap on a tap, same as a stick's hat); trim/flaps are
  suppressed while L3 is held.
- **Tests:** `CockpitLookUnsampledTests` (4, Ty's gain 4.9: placeholder zero → still straight ahead; tilt 5° → 24.5°
  after the real zero; mid-flight placeholder ignored; what counts as unsampled). `APhoneLaidFlat…` now uses a real
  flat reading instead of the bit-exact placeholder. CockpitLook / CockpitEye / GamepadShaping: 200 pass.

### 2026-11-02 (later) — Claude · lane/claude-gamepad-ios · gamepad: hot-plug, and the mapping turned round
**Ty: the 8BitDo works after relaunching with the pad connected; "the mapping is backwards".** The first build
had been installed while the phone was locked, so his relaunch was very likely the new build's FIRST launch.

**Hot-plug, checked in the code rather than assumed.** UnityFramework itself observes
`GCControllerDidConnectNotification` / `DidDisconnect` (both strings are in the shipped binary), and the Input
System 1.20 makes an added pad `Gamepad.current` at once (`InputManager.cs:1483`), hands `current` to the next pad
of its type when one is removed (`:1632`), and re-enables pads it disabled on focus loss when focus returns
(`InputManager.LegacyFocusHandling.cs`). So a pad paired while the game runs arrives without a relaunch. The gap
was ours: `GamepadInput` trusted `Gamepad.current` alone, which is re-pointed only on input / add / remove and
could be left on a pad that is disabled while another live one sits idle. `GamepadInput.Pad()` now takes
`current` if it is added and enabled, otherwise the live pad in `Gamepad.all` that spoke most recently, and the
log reports enable / disable as well as connect / disconnect, with the pad count.

**The mapping (shared code: iOS, Mac, Quest).** `GamepadShaping.Rudder(leftStickX, LT, RT)`,
`GamepadShaping.ThrottleRate`, `GamepadShaping.Lever` (replaces `Ramp`).

| Pad control | Does |
|---|---|
| RIGHT stick X | roll (aileron), right = roll right — expo 0.65 |
| RIGHT stick Y | pitch: pull back = nose UP, push = nose down (InvertElevator flips it, as for touch) — expo 0.65 |
| LEFT stick X | rudder — expo 0.65 |
| RT / LT | right / left rudder (RT − LT), summed with left stick X, clamped ±1 |
| LEFT stick Y | throttle as a RATE: up = power on, down = power off, centre = holds. Linear, dead zone 0.07, no expo; full stick moves the lever idle→full in 1.5 s; stops at idle |
| RB / LB (held) | throttle up / down as well, fixed 0.8 lever/s |
| D-pad up / down (held) | pitch trim nose down / nose up |
| D-pad right / left | flaps one step down / up (0 / 50 / 100 %) |
| A (held) | guns (combat aircraft) |
| B | smoke on / off |
| X (held) | wheel brakes |
| Y | change view |
| Menu (Start, ≡) | open / close OPTIONS |
| View (Select, ⧉) | REC: start / stop recording |

**Why a rate, not a position, for the throttle.** A gamepad stick springs back to centre the moment the thumb
lifts. Read as a position, centre would be half power, so every time the pilot let go to press a button the power
would snap to half, and holding full power would mean holding the stick on its stop for the whole flight. Read
as a rate, it behaves like a real throttle: it stays where it was set. The left stick's two axes use separate
per-axis dead zones rather than one radial zone, because they are separate controls (rudder and power), and a
radial zone would let a big rudder input leak a small throttle change.

**Quest.** The Quest branches (`lane/claude-vr-quest`, `lane/codex-vr-quest`, `lane/vr-cockpit-combined`) add
`TouchFlightControls` too (SceneBootstrap:75, mobile path), so `GamepadFlight` runs there, but they are on
`activeInputHandler: 0`, so `ENABLE_INPUT_SYSTEM` is off and `GamepadInput` compiles to its idle stub. They need
this lane's commits (75b275b and the follow-up), INCLUDING the `activeInputHandler: 2` line. Their Touch
controllers go through `UnityEngine.XR.InputDevices` and are not `Gamepad`s, so the two do not collide. Not
built for Quest here.

### 2026-11-02 — Claude · lane/claude-gamepad-ios · gamepads (8BitDo Pro 2 / MFi) fly the aeroplane on iOS
**Ty: an 8BitDo Pro 2 paired to the iPhone, "nothing works."**

**Root cause.** On iPhone / iPad the ONLY path from any joystick to the controls was
`TouchFlightControls.MobileJoystick()`, and its first line (`TouchFlightControls.cs:1017` at efdbb52) was
`if (jp == null || JoystickInput.SetupActive) return;` — no flight axis is read unless a joystick PROFILE exists
(OPTIONS › JOYSTICK SETUP). A freshly paired pad has none, so sticks, triggers and every flying button did
nothing. There was no default gamepad mapping anywhere. Behind it: the project was on the old Input Manager only
(`ProjectSettings activeInputHandler: 0`, no `com.unity.inputsystem` in `Packages/manifest.json`), which sees a
pad only as numbered axes `WJoy1…8` and `JoystickButton0…19` (`JoystickSetup.cs:70, 98`) with no names for
"left stick" or "Menu". The only pad code that reached iOS at all was the guns line reading legacy button numbers
(`TouchFlightControls.cs:517`). No Info.plist key is REQUIRED for iOS to deliver pad input; none was the cause.

**Fix.** Unity Input System 1.20.0 (bundled with the 6000.3.23f1 editor, so no download) with
`activeInputHandler: 2` (Both), so every existing `Input.*` call — touches, gyro, keys — is unchanged. Chosen over
a GameController-framework plugin because the Input System already wraps GCController on iOS, names every control
the same on MFi / Xbox / PlayStation / 8BitDo pads, and handles connect / disconnect itself; a native plugin would
be new Objective-C to maintain for the same thing.
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/GamepadShaping.cs` (pure): radial stick dead zone 0.07, trigger dead zone 0.05, cubic expo
  `(1−e)x + e·x³` with e = 0.65, rescaled so the stops are exactly ±1; rudder = RT − LT plus right-stick X, each
  shaped then summed and clamped; throttle ramp. Also pre-compensates the aeroplane's own dead zone
  (`Aircraft.ShapeAxis`, 0.02–0.05), so the pad does not stack two dead zones — the problem the touch pads hit
  before. The aeroplane's own EXPO (0.1–0.3) still applies on top: deliberate, noted for Ty.
- `Bridge/GamepadInput.cs`: reads `Gamepad.current` once a frame; disconnected reads as idle, which releases the
  controls on the next frame; logs `[Gamepad] connected / disconnected`.
- `TouchFlightControls.GamepadFlight()` runs after touch / keyboard / calibrated joystick: the pad drives a group
  (stick; rudder) only while it is moving it plus one frame to centre; idle, touch works as before; a finger on a
  pad always wins that pad's controls. `GamepadMenuButtons()` runs before the menu early-return so Menu can close
  what it opened. Legacy joystick-button guns only count when no Input System pad is connected (those numbers
  are RB / Menu / d-pad on iOS). `OptionsPanel.Toggle()` added.
- `BuildScript.OnPostProcessBuild`: `GCSupportsControllerUserInteraction = true`, `GCSupportedGameControllers =
  [ExtendedGamepad]`, `GameController.framework` (Unity already weak-links it; confirmed with `otool -L`).
- `tools/deploy/deploy.sh`: `set +e` before the install loop. The comment said the installs were not under
  `set -e`, but they were: a locked iPad Air's failing `OUT=$(devicectl …)` killed the script silently and the
  iPhone after it was never tried.

*(Superseded by the later entry above: the right stick flies now.)*

| Pad control | Does |
|---|---|
| Left stick X | roll (aileron), right = roll right |
| Left stick Y | pitch: pull back / down = nose UP, push = nose down (InvertElevator flips it, as for touch) |
| RT / LT | right / left rudder (RT − LT) |
| Right stick X | rudder, summed with the triggers, clamped ±1 |
| RB / LB (held) | throttle up / down, ramping (~0.8 lever/s); LB stops at idle |
| D-pad up / down (held) | pitch trim nose down / nose up |
| D-pad right / left | flaps one step down / up (0 / 50 / 100 %) |
| A (held) | guns (combat aircraft) |
| B | smoke on / off |
| X (held) | wheel brakes |
| Y | change view |
| Menu (Start, ≡) | open / close OPTIONS (pauses as the button does) |
| View (Select, ⧉) | REC: start / stop recording (`ClipRecorder.ToggleRecording`) |

**Tests.** `GamepadShapingTests` 34/34 (dead zone, radial zone, expo literals, full deflection for any e,
monotonic + odd over the whole travel, sign, rudder sum/clamp, dead-zone pre-compensation, ramp).

**Build.** `zsh tools/deploy/deploy.sh`: Unity export 0 `error CS`, `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, bundleVersion
`2026.1102.1441`. The script then died at the first device (above), so I installed the same .app by hand:
**Ty's iPhone — installed** (launch refused: "device was not, or could not be, unlocked"); **Ty's iPad mini —
installed and launched**; **Frisby_801C_EFB (iPad Air) — FAILED**: "The operation failed because the device was
still locked" (CoreDeviceError 10003). bundleVersion bump reverted; `activeInputHandler: 2` kept. Not verified
with a pad in hand.

### 2026-11-01 — Claude · lane/claude-loading-arms · the published arms, wired

**Branched from lane/claude-island-nav (69089df) and fast-forwarded Codex's table lane (ee088db) in first.**

**What this is.** Codex measured seat, fuel and baggage arms for all 34 configs and marked each row CONFIRMED
(a published TCDS/POH/W&B arm, cited, converted to this frame through a named anchor) or ESTIMATED (a seated
1.778 m de Leva mannequin, or geometry heuristics). This lane wires the `primary_body_m` column into the eight
configs that actually carry a `loading` block, and into their StreamingAssets mirrors.

**Why it is safe to move a station.** `AircraftLoad` solves the empty airframe BACKWARDS from the authored mass,
CG and inertia and the reference stations, so moving a station re-solves the empty CG to match and the reference
loading still lands exactly on the authored figures. That is not careful authoring, it is construction — which is
why a W&B edit of this size can land without retuning a single book speed. `referenceKg` was not touched
anywhere, and every new tank and baggage station went in at `referenceKg` 0, so a type that never burned fuel
still spawns dry and still burns nothing.

**The four placeholders are gone.** The 2-33, Pitts, Extra and Great Lakes had `station` set equal to the EYE,
because no W&B arm existed for them anywhere in the repo. They now carry published arms, and the big move is in
z: an eye is a head, a station is the mass centre of a seated body about half a metre lower.

**What the tests pin.** `ArmTable` in AircraftLoadTests repeats all 35 stations by hand — a test that reads the
file it is checking passes whatever the file says. Beside it: every station inside its own airframe (fuselage
extent from `crossflow`, semi-span from the wing strips), the reference loading reproducing the authored
aeroplane per type, the tandems' rear station aft of the front one and both on the centreline, and the new
tanks and baggage empty at spawn.

**One live bug found on the way.** The J-3's legacy tank entry shares the `tanks` key with the current schema
but carries none of its fields, so it was deserialising into a phantom fuel station at the origin with a zero
placard. Its legacy keys are kept; the live ones now sit beside them. See NEEDS YOU — it is the open schema
question with a worked example attached, not something to settle in a wiring lane.

### 2026-10-31 — Claude · lane/claude-eyepoints · the camera sits on the pilot's eye

**Three commits: 9594128 the eyes and a mass bug, 0d4884d the Great Lakes sights on the corrected eye,
229070a the camera and the look control. Built on lane/claude-island-nav at 6883ea4 (sights merged).**

**What was wrong.** Nothing carried an eye — no config had one and no model had an Eye node — so the Cockpit
view estimated one from the procedural canopy. The Unity log says how far out it was: the Extra's camera at
(-0.280, 0, -0.900) against an eye at (-1.597, 0, -0.518), **1.32 m forward and 0.38 m high**; the Pitts
(-0.645, 0, -0.900) against (-1.325, 0, -0.300). On every type but the 172 that put the camera above the
canopy, the wing or the cabin roof.

**The eye lives on the SEAT**, not in a table of its own, because it is the same seat: one authored entry
gives the loading page its mass station and the cockpit view its view point, so they cannot drift apart. The
PRIMARY seat is FIRST in the list, since CockpitView starts at SeatIndex 0 — rear on the tandems, front on the
2-33, left on the 172.

**Camera vs eye, all fourteen seats: 0.000 mm.** Near clip 0.050 m on each. `CockpitEyeCheck` drives the real
ChaseCamera path rather than recomputing the answer, and writes 42 review shots to `logs/cockpit/` (ahead and
both 90-degree snaps per seat, sights bound). The Pitts rear look-left shot shows all four bars crossing at one
centre on the horizon.

**Two of my own checks were wrong before they were right, and both failure modes are worth keeping.** The
first reported the camera 400–1300 mm off, which reads exactly like the bug under test; it was the harness,
which had not given the driver a sim, so CockpitView saw no seats and the camera fell back — a check that
builds the failing condition itself agrees with you whatever the code does. The second asserted "the eye is not
inside any mesh" and failed all fourteen: the drawn fuselage is a closed body of revolution with no cockpit
cutout, and an enclosed cabin is a shell the pilot is legitimately inside. That question is answered against
the real GLBs by check_sights instead.

**The look control did not exist.** CockpitLook has had ±90° snaps, a held pan and over-the-shoulder since the
seat work, and `CockpitView.OnScreenHat` was the channel for driving it from glass — **nothing ever wrote to
it**. On a device the only ways to turn your head were a stick's hat or the gyro goggles, which is why the
sights were unreachable: they sit on the eye's own lateral line, 1.85 m out on the Pitts and 3.94 m on the
Extra, about 90° off the nose. Three buttons now sit at the top centre in the cockpit, feeding OnScreenHat so
glass and hat share one tap-versus-pan window.

**The Great Lakes sights were built on the superseded eye.** check_sights cross-checks sights-spec.json against
`docs/models/eyepoints.json`, which did not exist here — so it was falling back to `git show aab015b:...`, the
unapproved lane whose own file says `"status": "proposed"`. That file now exists on this branch as the approved
record (Ty's v5, Codex's v4 kept beside it). Only one of twelve entries differs: great-lakes rear, by **0.2232
m**, matching Ty's "0.223 m low" exactly. Rebuilding its bars on the corrected eye turned
`FLAG: blocks 808 of 808 true-eye rays` into `PASS: 808/808`.

**A mass bug found by the new tests, not caused by this lane.** `loading.emptyMassKg` means "empty weight" in
the older W&B schema and "what is left when every station is empty" in the current one. Three types had the
older key and no current stations, so every spawn loaded them to their empty weight: Great Lakes −258 kg,
J-3 −206 kg, Swift −50 kg. Details and the open decision are in NEEDS YOU.

**Gates.** check_sights PASS on all 8 GLBs plus 808/808 visibility; SightsVisibilityCheck PASS (0 failures);
Unity batch 0 `error CS`; CockpitEyeCheck PASS (0 failures); the ignore guard printed nothing before every
commit. check_iac_assets.py is RED, inherited, and left red on purpose (NEEDS YOU 6). No gate is marked
approved.

### 2026-10-31 — Codex · lane/codex-island-world · indexed water and analytic terrain normal

**Status: implementation validated; `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **` (build 2026.1031.1125); committed locally. No gate approved.**
Inside the Codex sandbox, deploy.sh could not run Unity (Package Manager `listen EPERM` on `/tmp/Unity-Upm-51954.sock`),
so the build was run outside the sandbox on 2026-10-31: `tools/deploy/deploy.sh` exported at 11:24-11:26 PDT, xcodebuild
reported `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, and the app installed and launched on Ty's iPad mini at 11:29 AM PDT. The iPhone install
started at 11:29:24 but reported no result and the phone then showed unavailable: iPhone install NOT confirmed.
No push, merge, split-surfaces change, or Claude-lane/worktree edit.
No files are staged. The pre-existing ProjectSettings and StreamingAssets working-tree changes remain outside this work.

`WaterAt` now uses ordered 0.5 km cells for lake ellipses, water lanes, stream paths, outwash and dry-pad exclusions.
Canyon paths reuse the bounded 0.125 km geometry index with precomputed segment lengths; there is no whole-world
fallback. Per-cell maxima are 3 lakes, 1 water lane, 2 streams, 1 outwash feature and 3 dry pads. Cavity definitions
are cached, eliminating repeated allocating feature lookups on the height-aware path. The original water/stream
scan is frozen as `WaterAtReference` for independent parity checks. No authored world value changed.

**Claude API:** `WorldTerrain.GroundNormalAt(x,y)` returns the outward NED unit normal for precisely the upper
surface `GroundHeightAt` returns, with flat ground exactly `(0,0,-1)`. `WorldTerrain.IsWallAt(x,y,slopeTangent)`
compares slope without vector construction/normalization. `IslandTerrain.HeightAndGradientAt` returns height and
both sim-metre derivatives together. Existing `WorldTerrain.NormalAt` also uses this analytic path. The analytic
expressions are generated directly from `IslandHeightIndex.cs`; scalar and derivative evaluation share the same
precomputed nearest-segment/ray cells. `python3 tools/island/generate_gradient.py --check` and a source-hash test
reject stale generation. At a crease/tie the selected branch is used; a clamped endpoint/zero norm uses zero
derivative. A true jump has no unique normal; this reports the chosen surface's one-sided derivative. There are
no finite differences or displaced height queries in the production normal/predicate.

**Validation:** all 8 targeted tests pass. Water: 344,990 bitwise comparisons, including 100,000 uniform-area random
points in the full 20 km ring with and without `up`, every lake centre/rim, all water lanes and dry-pad exclusions,
every canyon segment and stream edge, outwash, grid seams, 3,803 reached cavity floors, sea and dry null cases.
Gradient: 168,926 bitwise height comparisons and 30,469 smooth derivative checks; known strip slope, unit length,
NED signs, inactive/sea flats and wall thresholds checked. The existing 46,161 exact height comparisons still pass.
Fresh water, normal, predicate and both cavity-floor paths allocate **zero bytes after initialization**.

Matched arm64 macOS / .NET 8.0.30 Release probes, tiered compilation disabled, five trials with fresh exact
coordinates per evaluator/trial (median microseconds; these are not device/IL2CPP measurements):

| Location | Old water scan | Indexed water | Analytic normal |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Full 20 km ring | 1.622 | 0.510 | 0.508 |
| Sea | 0.955 | 0.225 | 0.144 |
| KSNA | 2.538 | 0.535 | 1.031 |

**Cliff answer:** authored Bighorn Crown high-lake bank, not an index artefact. The bank's `basin_detail` blends
from the 7,840 ft lake/ridge down to the surrounding 1,402 m terrain within its 40 m authored bank envelope.
All 31 samples at N=3245 m, E=870..900 m match the pre-index evaluator bit for bit. It is a steep continuous blend;
the earlier coarse continuity sweep was not a bound on its local slope. No terrain/world repair is made here.

[Structured validation/build record](docs/pending/island-water-normal-checks.json) ·
[Full fresh-query timings and cliff profile](docs/pending/island-water-normal-cost.txt) ·

### 2026-10-29 — Claude · lane/claude-glacier-ski · glacier ski landing zones, sim side

Picked up the visual owner's HANDOFF "glacier ski landing zones" (P4-M7 table and its measured CSV). Branched
off lane/codex-visuals so the heights and the shader masks come from one geometry; **nothing pushed or merged**.

- **THE KNIK COULD NOT BE GRADED AS A BENCH, and that is the headline.** Measured on a 10 m grid: the clear
  rectangle runs 1690.3 m down to 1538.5 m — **152 m of fall in 800 m, 27.5° at its upper end** — and its lower
  end is **67 m from the ice front**, with the proglacial lake beyond. So the fill has nowhere to go, and a 3°
  bench touching natural ground at the bottom needs a **110 m cut** at the top: a slot canyon in the ice. The
  cause is that `Glacier.IceHeightAt` used `(1−s)³` of the whole drop while its own comment claimed it put
  "nearly all of the drop in the icefall at the top and then a long shallow tongue". A cubic has no tongue —
  its gradient falls off smoothly the whole way — so the LZ straddled the foot of the icefall.
- **Fixed by giving the glaciers the profile that comment describes**: below s = 0.30 the ice falls at exactly
  2.8°; above it the icefall carries all the rest at a constant gradient with a 0.04-in-s fillet where the two
  meet. Head, terminus, length, width, total drop and both lakes are untouched. Steepest point: **Knik 53.5°,
  Colony 52.9°, against the 51.3° / 50.7° the cubic already reached at the head** — nowhere more than ~2°
  steeper than what it replaces, while the whole lower 70% is landable instead of the last few hundred metres.
  Both profiles verified monotone over 500 samples.
- **The three zones, measured (10 m grid, spans confined to the rectangle):**

| Zone | Grade | Along | Across | 30 m residual | Fall over 800 m |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Knik | its own tongue | **2.80°** | 0.00° | 0.00 m | 39.1 m (1541.3–1580.4) |
| Colony | pad, flattened | **0.00°** | 0.00° | 0.00 m | 0.0 m (1524.0) |
| East Fork | pad, already flat | **0.00°** | 0.00° | 0.00 m | 0.0 m (1524.0) |

  All inside 3° / 2° / 0.5 m. The height field is within **0.0 cm** of each zone's own declared plane.
  Measuring the slope over spans that reached out into the apron instead made the Knik read 5.57° — the apron,
  not the thing that was graded; the test now measures it the way the tolerance is stated.
- **The zones are applied LAST, after the lakes.** The Knik's lake reaches r = 1.47 of its own radius at the
  zone's lower end and its shore blend was taking 3 m off the last 40 m of the rectangle — a 4° break across
  the touchdown end. Nothing may be laid over a graded surface afterwards.
- **Buffer and crevasse no-go areas.** 920 × 200 m, with the earthwork blended out over the 60 m along / 60 m
  across between the two rectangles — the same feather the terrain shader uses to exclude its cracks. 987
  points per buffer verified crevasse-free; the icefall (upper 55%), the margins (|U| > 0.45 × half-width) and
  local steepening past 12° are marked. Broken ice is something the wheels can FEEL: 0.58 m blocks at a few
  metres' pitch outside the buffer, so a no-go area is not a decoration. These are not modelled crevasses —
  nothing swallows you, and **whether it should is Ty's call** (see NEEDS YOU).
- **`PackedGlacierSnow`**, appended as value 5 after auditing serialisation (`ChartRaster` stores the enum as a
  byte, so members may be added but never renumbered; it has its own chart colour). Inside the safety buffer;
  the rest of the ice stays `Snow`. Coefficients as the handoff proposed: **ski rolling 0.02** (soft snow 0.055),
  **ski lateral 0.12** (soft snow 0.05), **keel/skid sliding 0.04**, **wheel rolling 0.18** rising to **0.35**
  with sinkage — driven by the wheel's own bearing pressure, so a bushwheel at 2.6 kN sits at 0.21 and a hard
  tyre at 12 kN is at the full 0.35. The ski's lateral hold is now an absolute μ of its own rather than a scale
  on the tyre's, because what resists a sideways slide is the ski's edge and the tyre inside it is irrelevant.
- **The East Fork is in Core now.** Its axis and half-width were a literal in `AlaskaBuilder.GlacierAxis`
  (`Vector4(7500, −7000, 7700, −5500)`, 280 m) that the sim could not see — so the shader painted ice along a
  corridor the surface model called gravel, and a pilot could land on what looked like a glacier and find rock.
  All three corridors come from `AlaskaRange.Corridors`; it is a corridor and not a `Glacier`, because it has no
  head on the mountain, no icefall and no proglacial lake, and the tests that ask every `Glacier` for its lake
  and its gradient still mean what they say.
- **Peaks: ridges and buttresses.** The reach now varies with the bearing — 3–5 main ridges per peak with
  cirques bitten in between, so the foot is a star and not a circle — plus a broad rib standing out on the
  lower flank of each ridge. The reach only ever varies DOWNWARD, never past `RadiusM`: Foraker's skirt already
  reaches 1,050 m of the 1,200 m south pad, and letting a ridge reach further brings back the 60 m step along
  y = Y0 − 900 that the world sweep found on 2026-10-17.
- **Alpine treeline forest.** One line in `Forest.Timbered` — `SurfaceAt(x, y) != Surface.Rough` — kept every
  conifer off the Cascades, because the alpine block reports Gravel as a whole ("granite and scree, not
  pasture"). That is why render after render came back with bare rock to the valley floor and a "treeline" that
  was only the edge of the block. The alpine block is now allowed its timber and `TreeLineAt` decides where it
  ends. It needed a second piece: the Summit plateau is at 2,134 m and the treeline over that longitude comes
  out near 2,070 m — *below the valley floors* — so allowing timber alone would have put nothing on it but a
  fringe. `AlpineMassLiftM` adds 400 m over the massif, fading over 1,800 m outside it: the mass-elevation
  (Massenerhebung) effect, which is real and named. The line then runs near 2,480 m, which puts timber across
  the floors and up the first 350 m of every peak and leaves bare rock above. **Alaska is deliberately NOT
  given forest**: it is glacier country, and what is not ice there is moraine and outwash.
- **Verified by ski-landing on each glacier**, flown, Cub on skis, touchdown 31 kt up the ice 40 m inside the
  downhill end: Knik **270 m, climbing 13.1 m**, stopped at S+91 / U−25.2; Colony and East Fork **313 m** on the
  flat, stopped at S+47 / U−22.5. All three stop inside the 800 m rectangle and inside the 80 m width. Two
  things this test caught about itself and not about the zones: with the rudder free the Cub wandered 55–68 m
  off the centreline (a ski has a fraction of a tyre's lateral hold and the tailwheel castors), so there is a
  pilot on the rudder; and the first pilot read its heading out of `RigidBodyState.Velocity`, which is in BODY
  axes — the track came out as the sideslip direction, the rudder sat at full deflection for the whole run, and
  **two different gain sets produced byte-identical results**, which is how it was found.
- **Four things the suite then caught, all of them real and none of them in the glacier work proper.**
  1. `AtmosphereField` switches on the surface with a `_ => 0.4` default, so the new surface fell through to
     open-country heating and **a glacier landing zone would have generated thermals like a pasture**. It gets
     the ice's own −0.5. Found by auditing every switch over `Surface` for a default arm rather than by a test.
  2. `WorldContinuityTests.NowhereDoesTheGroundStopDead` found **15 m in 0.1 m at (2520, −6820)**, on Hunter's
     west flank. The step is in the BASE FIELD — an 18 m escarpment — and `cone = baseH + (TopM − baseH) ×
     Smooth(t)` was carrying a scaled copy of it up the mountainside as an 11 m wall. The old fixed-radius
     footprint happened to attenuate it below the test's threshold; varying the reach with the bearing pushed
     it over. The cone now stands on `BaseM + ReliefM × Smooth(t)` — the plateau the range's own doc has always
     said its heights are relief above — so no wrinkle in the base field propagates into it at all. Plus a fade
     over the last 6% of the reach, because the `Ridge` term is multiplied by `(0.3 + 0.7 sin(πt))`, which is
     0.3 and **not zero** at the rim, so a cone could stand up to 26 m proud of the ground at its own edge.
  3. **A ski had no static friction**, and it turns out to matter. The Knik's graded tongue falls at 2.8°
     (tan = 0.049) and a ski's running coefficient on packed snow is 0.02, so an aeroplane that had stopped
     crept back down the zone for ever. That is the model saying you cannot park on a glacier, and a ski pilot
     parks on one. A ski base rides on a film of water it melts itself and there is no film at a standstill —
     published static coefficients run 0.1–0.4 against 0.02–0.08 kinetic — so the coefficient is now 4× its
     running value at rest, fading to the running value by 1 m/s. 0.08 on packed snow against the 0.049 it has
     to hold.
  4. `ACubOnSkisCanBePutOnTheKnikAndStop` then wanted **60 s instead of 40**: it stops at 40.3 s, where it used
     to stop at 32, because it is now landing on the graded surface where the ski runs more than twice as
     freely. Nothing else about it changed — 198 m against its 400 m limit, climbing 9.0 m.
  Two tests were updated rather than fixed, and for the same reason in both: the middle of a 1,334 m glacier
  and the Knik touchdown point at s = 0.86 both fall inside a landing zone's safety buffer, so they report
  `PackedGlacierSnow` and not `Snow`. `AlaskaTests` now asks that ice be snow of one kind or the other, which
  is what that test is about; `WheelSkiTests` asserts the packed zone by name, because that is the surface the
  landing is now being made on and that is the point.
- **11 new tests, all passing.** Suite: **787 passed, 4 failed, 3 skipped, 794 total** — the four are the known
  ones by name (flat-spin smoke, Pitts three-point and wheel landings ×2, Pitts quick feet). Nothing new.
  (The 794 is a counted discovery, not a remembered number: I first wrote 789/796 into this entry from
  arithmetic rather than from the run, which is the same habit that put `init` in a commit that did not build.)
- **A fifth failure turned out to be older than this lane, and it is a shipping bug.**
  `GlideTableTests.EveryAircraftPassesTheNewAirplaneProtocol` reported "great-lakes-like: StreamingAssets copy
  missing or different". I checked it on clean `split-surfaces` in the main checkout and it fails there too, so
  **yesterday's "4-failure baseline" is 5 today** and I was wrong to carry 4 forward without re-checking.
  `unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/aircraft/` is **gitignored**, yet 62 of the 63 aircraft configs are tracked in
  it — they are the data the GAME loads at runtime, mirrored from `configs/aircraft` by `Editor/ConfigSync.cs`.
  The Great Lakes' mirror was never added, and because the directory is ignored a plain `git add` would have
  skipped it silently. So the aeroplane's runtime config existed only as an untracked file created by a Unity
  run, and it reached the shipped ipa by luck. Added with `git add -f`, with a `.meta` carrying a GUID derived
  from the asset path so it is the same in every worktree — an untracked `.meta` is the trap where Unity
  invents a fresh GUID. **Ty: this wants the same fix on `split-surfaces` and `main`, and the ignore rule
  wants a decision — either these mirrors are tracked, in which case the rule should not cover them, or they
  are generated, in which case 62 of them should not be in git.**

**For the visual owner:** the sim's crevasse no-go is WIDER than the cracks the terrain shader draws — the
shader gates on the outer 35% of the half-width and the upper 35% of the length, the sim on the outer 55% and
the upper 55%. The sim's is deliberately the more conservative of the two, but the drawn cracks should grow to
match it or a pilot will see clean ice where the ground is broken. Also: the Knik's ice is now ~140 m lower at
s = 0.30 than it was, so its trough is 11% deeper over the tongue, and the Colony pad's upper approach has a
natural wall just outside the buffer (35° at S −450) — it is landed from down-glacier, uphill, into the glacier
wind, which is the technique anyway.
### 2026-10-29 — Claude · lane/claude-urban-collision · the cities' low-rise is something you crash into

Owner: "Give them collision so aircraft crash into them, using simple box or roof-prism colliders generated
from the same placement data. Keep landing pads, rings and approach paths clear." Branched off the TIP of
`lane/codex-visuals` (`192070e`, C13), not `8debd22` — `UrbanFacades.cs` gained 405 lines between the two,
and colliders have to match the layout that is actually shipping.

**The placement is not re-derived.** `RoofDistrict` is six hundred lines of stateful placement gated on
terrain, forest and a camera's lens, rewritten twice today. A copy of that layout in Core would be wrong
inside a week. Instead `RoofDistrict.Building` registers each house through `UrbanLowRise.Add` **from the
same `foot` and `size` that make the mesh** — 13 lines added to Codex's file, and collision follows him
wherever he moves a building. What is in Core is the half that is physics:

- `src/FlyingGame.Core/UrbanLowRise.cs` — a block is a box to the eave with a 36° gable above it, ridged
  along its long axis. Penetration takes the least-penetration face **but never the floor**: a house stands
  ON the ground, and a free-standing box's blind least-penetration rule drives an aeroplane that clipped a
  wall a metre up into the earth. Walls are footed 2 m down so a wheel at the surface is inside the house.
  A roof strike pushes off the **slope** of the gable, not straight up.
- The index. Three cities is ~1,600 buildings; a linear scan at 20 hard points x 500 Hz would be most of a
  step. A 64 m uniform grid in compressed-row form measures **1.1 µs per step** against a 2,000 µs budget,
  and a test checks it against a plain scan over 40,000 samples (0 disagreements).
- Keep-clear, staked lazily out of Core's own data on the first `Add`.

**Two mistakes worth recording, both caught by building the world rather than by the unit tests.**

1. *The Valley cities' houses were being wiped.* `WorldBuilder.BuildAll` draws the plateau cities inside
   `BuildLandmarks`, **before** it calls `Landmarks.RegisterSolids`. Clearing the registry there deleted
   1,166 buildings and left only the combat city's 408; the keep-clear rules arrived equally late. The
   registry is now emptied once at the top of `BuildAll` and the rules stake themselves on first use, so no
   build order can get between them.
2. *Keeping the airport PADS clear was the wrong rule.* A pad is 2,950 x 1,700 m of flattened field around
   30 m of runway. Refusing the rectangle threw out **138 houses per Valley city** standing a kilometre from
   any strip, and a 4 km final (instrument dimensions, in a world 8.6 x 14.9 NM end to end) ate 71 more in
   the combat city, which sits on the Valley's extended centreline. The rule now walks
   `WorldTerrain.AirportStrips` — main, crosswind, gravel, grass, all axis-aligned — at 120 m either side,
   plus a 1,500 m x ±150 m final off each end of the **paved** runways only. Which houses belong on an
   airfield is the visual owner's call; which ground an aeroplane lands on is not.

**Measured in the built world** (`UrbanCollisionCheck`, a new editor tool, `-nographics`):

| | |
|---|---|
| registered as solids | **1,639** (Valley 640-ish, Bench 520-ish, combat 479) |
| refused | **6** — 4 Valley paved final south, 2 city pad approach |
| standing where a pilot needs the ground | **0** |
| tallest / widest | 14.4 m to the ridge / 52.0 m |
| 2.8 km run at 4 m AGL through a city | inside a building for **199 m** |

Suite **783 passed / 4 failed / 3 skipped / 790 total** — the four are the known baseline (three Pitts
ground-handling, `SmokeGoesRoundTheAirframeInTheFlatSpin`). Seven new tests in
`tools/FlightTests/UrbanLowRiseTests.cs`. Unity Bridge and Core both compile clean. Lane only; not pushed,
not merged.

### 2026-10-29 — Claude · lane/claude-extra-hinges · the propeller turns about its spinner

Ty: "The Extra 300's propeller spins about a point 0.24 m above its real axis — the prop looks too high."
Exactly right, to the centimetre. `AirframeVisual.CarveModel` centred each hub on the middle of the
propeller's bounding box. With three blades one of them points straight up, so the box ran -0.52..+1.00 m
vertically and its centre sat 0.24 m high; the earlier tip snap had already put the hub on the axis, and
this pass undid it. The prop swept a cone, and the blur disc hung above the cowl.

The hub now goes on the **spinner's own centroid** — the bound propeller vertices inside 0.4 R of the hub
the tip snap found — which is on the axis whatever the blade count. A model with no spinner falls back to
the mean of every bound propeller vertex, also on the axis for any evenly spaced blades. The 0.5 R reject
that throws out a wheel pant or a cowl piece is unchanged, and only x and y move; the hub keeps its z.

Measured with SKIN_DIAG, which now prints the new hub beside the box centre the old code would have used:

| | new hub | old box centre | blades |
|---|---|---|---|
| Extra 300 | (0.00, **0.00**, 2.54) | (0.00, **0.24**, 1.97) | 3 |
| Pitts S-2B | (0.00, 0.00, 1.68) | (0.00, 0.00, 1.69) | 2 |
| Great Lakes | (0.00, 0.20, 1.61) | (0.00, 0.20, 0.86) | 2 |

Both two-blade props are unchanged. The c172 and the Seminole bind no propeller from their models at all
(`0/1` and `0/2` propellers bound), so neither pass reaches them.

**Why it hid:** `AircraftRender` only ever rendered a stopped propeller — `SpinProps(_ => 0f, 0.02f)`. It
gains `-rpm <rpm>` and `-propdeg <deg>`, which hold a turning hub at a chosen angle (`SpinProps` integrates
rpm over dt, so `dt = deg / (rpm * 6)`), and `-own`, without which the c172 and Seminole GLBs cannot be
reached at all. Rendered at 0 rpm, at 300 rpm with the hub turned 60°, and at 2,500 rpm: the spinner stays
on the cowl nose and the blur disc is centred on it. Lane only; not pushed, not merged.

### 2026-10-30 — the rigs mirror stops being committed (Claude)

`unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs/` was gitignored **and** tracked on this lane — `docs/models/ea300-own-rig.json`
mirrored to `extra-300-like-own.json` — which is exactly the trap that shipped the Great Lakes with no runtime config.
In that state `git add` on a new mirror silently does nothing and only `git add -f` works, so a rebuilt or renamed
rig reaches the player only if somebody remembers the flag.

- `.gitignore`: `unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs/` and `rigs.meta`.
- `git rm -r --cached unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs.meta`. No `git add -f`
  anywhere; the files stay on disk and `ConfigSync` rewrites them on editor load, so the cockpit still animates.
- `rigs.meta` sits BESIDE the directory, not inside it, so `git rm -r --cached .../rigs` misses it. It took a second
  `git ls-files | git check-ignore --no-index --stdin -v` to notice. Mind the `--no-index`: without it `check-ignore`
  says nothing about tracked files, which is precisely the set in question.

Tracked-but-ignored on this lane is now the 62 aircraft configs and `build/cg-check.csv` only — both of which are
`lane/claude-config-mirror`'s to remove, by design. `StreamingAssetsTests` is deliberately NOT ported here: its
`NoTrackedFileIsGitignored` check would fail on those 62, and it arrives with the branch that deletes them.

**Why the rigs row lives on `config-mirror` and not here.** The destination name is not a mapping anybody should
type: a rig table declares the model it belongs to in its own `model` field, and that is what names the file the game
looks for. A fixed source→dest table would hold a second copy of something the file already says, and a rebuilt rig
that changed its model name would mirror to the old path and go on looking right. So `config-mirror`'s `Mirror` gained
a content resolver rather than a name field, and its row — `new("docs/models", "rigs", "*-rig.json", destGlob:
"*.json", nameFromContent: RigModelName)` — matches zero files on `split-surfaces` and picks this lane's rig up the
moment it merges. Verified there against this very file: it landed as `extra-300-like-own.json`, byte-identical,
and was swept by name when the source was removed.

**Gates.** Suite **4 failed / 790 passed / 3 skipped** — the baseline four (Pitts ground handling x3, flat-spin
smoke). That 4 is flattered, and worth knowing before anyone reads it as clean:
`EveryAircraftPassesTheNewAirplaneProtocol` passes here only because this worktree holds an **untracked**
`great-lakes-like.json` that a Unity run left behind. A fresh clone of this branch would show 5. That is the trap
itself, demonstrated one more time, and the reason `config-mirror` was measured in a brand-new worktree.
`** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, installed on the iPad mini, and the built player carries the rig at
`Data/Raw/rigs/extra-300-like-own.json`.

**Recommended merge order:** `config-mirror` → `flow-plates` → `extra-cockpit` → `sgs233-rig`. `config-mirror` first because it removes the aircraft trap and adds the tests everything else assumes; `flow-plates` second because it is independent and takes the failure baseline from 4 to 3, leaving only the three Pitts ground-handling tests awaiting your feel-test.

### 2026-10-30 — Claude · lane/claude-extra-cockpit · the Extra 300's cockpit is wired to the sim

Branched off `lane/codex-extra-cockpit` 073f113. Physics and configs untouched, as asked.

**The rig drives itself from the file.** The 2-33's rig machinery (`src/FlyingGame.Core/ModelRig.cs` +
`AirframeRig.cs`, from `lane/claude-sgs233-rig`) reads the Extra's table with no change at all -- the schema is
the same, so nothing was transcribed and a rebuilt model brings its own new rig. `RigInputs` grew from the
2-33's 11 params to the 33 this panel asks for; a param no rig names costs one unread field, and `TryGet` still
returns false for anything the sim cannot supply, so a rig that grows a param is REPORTED rather than silently
still. ConfigSync mirrors `docs/models/*-rig.json` into StreamingAssets keyed by the `model` field inside each
file.

**Measured, not eyeballed** (`OwnRigCheck`, generic so the Pitts and the Great Lakes can use it:
`-executeMethod FlyingGame.EditorTools.OwnRigCheck.Run`, `RIG_TYPE=` to pick a type, `RIG_SHOTS=<dir>` for
renders). 60/60 nodes bound, 0 off their pivot (worst: the canopy, 0.1 mm), 0 under the wrong parent, every
fixture present. Then each control to its stop, reported in the aeroplane's own frame:

    stick right      both grips 140/146 mm stbd; L aileron TE down, R TE up, 30 deg each
    stick aft        both grips 138/144 mm aft;  both elevator halves TE up 130 mm
    right pedal      L pedal 56 mm aft, R 52 mm fwd; rudder TE 267 mm to starboard
    left toe brake   only the two LEFT brake plates move, 20 mm fwd
    throttle open    both levers 122 mm fwd;  prop/mixture pulled: 50 mm aft
    canopy open      789 mm up and 835 mm to STARBOARD, on the right sill, both locks riding with it
    release pulled   both handles 40 mm inboard
    150 kt/3500 ft   ASI 180 deg, altimeter 3.5 turns, tach 205.7, MP 168, VSI 64, CHT 108, G +48/max 72/min -36

A warning about reading that output, written down because it cost an hour: the probe follows the vertex
FARTHEST FROM THE HINGE LINE, and on this model the ailerons' farthest vertex is 246 mm FORWARD of and 282 mm
below the hinge, not behind it. "The far point moved up" therefore means the trailing edge went DOWN. Taken at
face value it reads as a reversed aileron. It is not: the rig is correct, on every surface and every lever.

**What was built on top of the rig.**
- CAMERA: the cockpit view sits on the model's own eye point, `Eye_Rear` by default because solo aerobatics is
  flown from the back (the model marks it `default_view`), `Eye_Front` from OPTIONS > Display. The ORIENTATION
  still comes from the airframe, not from the empty: an eye point is a position, and a stray rotation on one
  would cant the whole view with nothing on screen to say why. A bail-out now also leaves from the real seat.
- DISPLAYS: the COM and transponder faces are drawn by generating a texture from a 5x7 font kept in
  `CockpitDisplays`, NOT by a TextMesh. The livery code already carries the scar -- "a TextMesh with a null font
  draws nothing" -- and on a cockpit LCD that would read as a dead radio with nothing in the log. The material
  is FlyingGame/Decal, already force-included in the player, which takes the image's luminance as alpha so the
  black field drops out onto the head's own dark face. Costs one small upload when the frequency changes.
- CANOPY: `canopy_open` / `canopy_lock` / `canopy_jettison` all driven. OPTIONS opens and shuts it below 60 kt
  (E -- the POH gives no figure); faster than that it shuts and locks itself. On a bail-out the REAL `Canopy`
  node leaves -- glass, frame and both lock levers, because they are its children in the GLB -- with the
  airframe's velocity and a hinge kick, the rig stops driving it, and the aeroplane stays canopy-less until the
  next flight's rebuild. The old procedural stand-in dome is still there for types without a rigged canopy.
- SEQUENCE CARD: generic, not the Extra's. Any model with a `SequenceCard_Face` node and material gets it, so
  the Pitts, the Great Lakes and the Decathlon need no code when Codex adds the holder. Pick a PDF
  (UIDocumentPickerViewController, PDFs only, asCopy), choose a page, PDFKit rasterises it LETTERBOXED onto a
  white 1275x1650 canvas and never stretches it; kept per aeroplane under
  `persistentDataPath/sequence-cards/<aircraftId>/` with the source PDF and page beside it, so turning a page
  does not need the picker again; "Default" puts the IAC card back. Presentation only -- nothing in Core or Sim
  knows the card exists. Off iOS there is no PDFKit, so the editor takes a PNG path instead (OPTIONS, the
  `SEQ_CARD` env var, or the editor's own file panel).

**Tests.** 14 in `tools/FlightTests/ModelRigTests.cs`: the map's clamping, wrapping, running past the ends and
lever notches; that a turn and a slide do NOT convert to Unity the same way; that every one of the 33 params is
readable; and the Extra's own table against the POH travels (aileron +-30, elevator +-25, rudder +-30), the
canopy being the one part that comes off, both seats being on the same controls, every child listed after its
parent, and every node's map reading zero at the param the model was drawn at -- which is the invariant the
whole driver rests on, since interp() is applied to the imported rest pose.

**Two bugs found in my own work, both of the kind a render cannot show.** `RigInputs` is a struct, so the
check's probe helper took `Action<RigInputs>` and every `r.Aileron = 1` was written to a copy: the first run
measured the parked cockpit against itself and printed eleven empty probe blocks. And the transponder read
"4321 AL" -- eight characters at the scale the frequency wanted overflowed a 156-px face -- so the glyph scale
is now fitted to the longest line instead of trusting a constant.

**Also fixed in passing:** the chain-perpendicularity rule (the one that found the 2-33's yaw-string fault) was
calling the Extra's two canopy lock levers broken. It only governs a TRUE chain, where parent and child move on
the SAME param; a part that merely rides on a moving parent and has a hinge of its own is free to sit anywhere.
Narrowed, and the 2-33's yaw string still fails it.

**And one that only the player build could find**, which is the whole reason for the deploy gate: the first
`deploy.sh` compiled everything and then failed at the LINK with `"_OBJC_CLASS_$_PDFDocument", referenced
from:` — Unity links neither PDFKit nor UniformTypeIdentifiers on its own, and nothing in the editor or the
test suite wants them. Both are now added in `BuildScript.OnPostProcessBuild` beside the clip encoder's.

**Seven tests had been red since the v15 cockpit landed, and nobody had noticed.** The suite on this lane came
back 11 failed. Running the same tests at `073f113` gave the same 11, and at `fe9c35e` — the v15 commit itself —
seven of them were already failing, all at one assertion in `OwnPittsModelTests.Vertices`: *"the build bakes
every transform into the mesh"*. For an unrigged model that was true. A RIGGED one cannot satisfy it: a rig
node's whole purpose is to sit on its own pivot with a transform of its own, so `GearMatchesTheModels` x3,
`EachLiveryHasItsOwnPropeller` x3 and `UnmarkedIsTheSameAirframeAsItsMarkedSibling` went red the moment the
cockpit arrived — not because anything about the aeroplane was wrong, but because the reader was asking for
something a rig is not allowed to give it. `Vertices` now composes each node's transform up to the scene root
(glTF `matrix`, or T * R * S) instead of forbidding transforms, which is what every caller wanted anyway: where
each part really is on the aeroplane. Seven green, the Pitts' unrigged GLBs unaffected (identity composes to
identity), and the suite is 4 failed / 790 passed / 3 skipped — the four being the Pitts ground handling (x3)
and the flat-spin smoke check, all already written up above and all deliberately not forced green.

Gates: suite 4 failed / 790 passed / 3 skipped (down from 11 failed), the Unity editor compiling all four
assemblies clean, and `tools/deploy/deploy.sh` to `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **` (build 2026.1030.0728, installed and
launched on the iPad mini). Not pushed, not merged.
