# Agent log — lane/claude-regional-atmosphere

## NEEDS YOU
Questions for Ty about the regional atmosphere. Nothing here blocks the branch; each item has a working default.
1. **The calm zone covers the foot of the first bluff.** The zone is a stadium round the Valley's main runway, radius 1,200 m. The first wall's toe is about 1,100 m west of the centreline, so the lower face of the wall is inside the calm column for the zone's 6 km length, below its 762 m MSL ceiling. Ridge lift above the crest and north and south of the zone is untouched. Three options: (a) keep it like this; (b) narrow the zone on the bluff side, with a right-hand pattern to the east only; (c) shorten it along the wall. Default: (a).
2. **Events.** Only free flight uses the regional air. Challenges, scored contests, lessons, practice and every `event:` (races, STOL, dogfight) fly the uniform air exactly as before. Should any events fly the regional air?
3. **"Turbulence: Calm" in regional mode** currently means "the regions' own turbulence" (×1). Light, Moderate and Severe scale it ×1.5, ×2.2 and ×3. Should Calm switch regional turbulence off entirely? The home field is calm either way.
4. **The menu temperature** sets the air mass's ISA deviation everywhere except the home field, which stays exactly ISA as asked. The spawn trim solver still uses the session deviation, so a hot-day spawn at home is trimmed for slightly thinner air than it meets. The difference is tiny. Is this OK?
5. **Altimeters** read true altitude. Regions now have their own QNH (±3 hPa). Should the altimeter read pressure altitude with a local QNH setting? The flight-test data block now shows the regional static pressure.
6. **Sim clock (pre-existing).** Every `Aircraft.Step` advances `Atmosphere.SimTimeSec`, AI aircraft included, so turbulence and the airport-2/3 wind processes run faster when there is AI traffic. This was already true of the old turbulence. Should one place own the clock?
7. **Airport 3's wind is from 060.** I chose 060 because the obvious north-westerly put Mesa in the lee of the wall 900 m west of it, and rotors would have changed the gusts' character. Do you prefer another direction?
8. **Mountain magnitudes.** In 23 kt over the Alaska range, the lee sink reaches about −3.5 m/s and σ about 4.7 m/s, which is moderate-to-severe. This is physically plausible but harsh. Should it be tuned down (`orographicScale`, `leeSinkFraction`)?
9. **Not flown in the editor or on a device.** Unity batchmode compiled everything cleanly, but nobody has looked at the J overlay, the moved Valley thermals or the feel in the air yet.
10. **Moved thermals.** The Valley's four hand-placed thermals and the crop-field one sat inside the zone or its blend band. They now start just outside it (~350–600 m further from the runway). Is that OK, or should they be re-authored?
11. **Rotor speeds in the big ranges look too strong (pre-existing, found while measuring the lakes).** In 25 kt, the worst rotor speed in the rough lee of the Alaska massif is about 74 m/s before lake smoothing. Over the Cascades' Outlet approach it is 53 m/s. Lake smoothing takes these to 26 and 19 m/s. The cause is the crest-level wind: it is referred to drops of up to ~1 km with the power-law profile, then multiplied by `orographicScale` 1.3 and the range's `speedScale`. Rotor speeds that high are not realistic. Should rotor speed be capped (for example at ~1.2 × the 10 m wind), or `rotorStrength` lowered? Default: unchanged, because that tuning is outside this task.
12. **The Alaska glacier lakes are smoothed but still rough-ish.** The rule is multiplicative: lake × mountain. The Knik lake sits under The High One (×1.85, blending to the range's ×1.6), so its core comes out about ×0.6. That is smoother than the standard ×1, but rougher than the Cascades' lakes (×0.30). Do you want lake cores to have an absolute ceiling instead, such as "never rougher than ×0.4"? Default: multiplicative, as the tests specify.
13. **Lakes you only fly into stay rough.** Hidden, Cirque and Crown are not `Landable`, so they get no smoothing. Is that right?

## Log
### 2026-10-27 — Claude · lane/claude-regional-atmosphere · rough and smooth mountains, smooth seaplane lakes
Ty: "I want some mountains to be rough and some to be more smooth; make the mountains with lakes to land seaplanes on smoother."

**What it does**
- `Orography.Evaluate(..., roughness)`: a new per-point factor. It multiplies the terrain's mechanical effects: the orographic σ (slope and lee/rotor turbulence), the rotor's spin and the lee downdraught.
- The laminar effects are not scaled: speed-up, upslope lift and lee waves. A smooth ridge soars exactly as well as a rough one, and a test pins that slope lift is identical.
- The factor is the mountain's own (`roughness` zones) × the seaplane-lake smoothing (`lakeSmoothing`).
- Each zone also has a `lengthScale`. It multiplies the turbulence length scales, weighted by the terrain's share of the turbulence variance. Rough means shorter, sharper eddies; smooth means longer ones. Air away from terrain is unchanged.
- No zones and no lake sources gives the old field bit for bit. All 19 earlier atmosphere tests pass untouched.
- `AtmosphereSample` gains `OrographicSigmaMs`, `RotorMs`, `RoughnessFactor` and `LakeSmoothing`. The J overlay shows them on a new readout line, and draws each lake's smooth core and approach corridors in blue.

**Mountains → roughness** (`configs/atmosphere/regional-atmosphere.json` → `roughness`)
- Layered in list order over ×1, each zone blending in on a smootherstep. Every shape is resolved from the terrain code (`WorldTerrain.EdgeMeanY`/`EscarpmentWidthM`/`FrontExtraWidthM`, the `AlaskaRange`/`AlpineRange`/`FrankChurch`/`HighDesert`/`Hills` block constants, `Coast.MeanShoreY`, the `Peaks` tables), so the zones move if the terrain does.
- Anything not listed (open plateau, the outwash plain) is ×1.

| zone | feature | factor | L× | character |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The High One summit | `peak:alaska:The High One` (radius 2,000 − 700 m) | **1.85** | 0.70 | roughest air in the world |
| Huntington | `peak:alaska:Huntington` | **1.75** | 0.75 | steep fluted ice |
| Alaska range | `alaskaRange` (+400 m) | **1.6** | 0.80 | ice and rock walls |
| Mesa→Summit wall | `front:2` | **1.5** | 0.80 | the top step, roughest wall |
| Frank Church canyon | `frankChurch` | **1.45** | 0.75 | canyon rotor |
| Foraker | `peak:alaska:Foraker` | 1.35 | 0.90 | broad, rounder |
| Dragontail | `peak:alpine:Dragontail` | 1.35 | 0.75 | the jagged one (over Hidden, not landable) |
| Bench→Mesa wall | `front:1` | 1.3 | 0.85 | broken rock under the Alaska range |
| Reno mesas | `highDesert` | 1.25 | 0.85 | sharp rims, talus |
| Cathedral | `peak:alpine:Cathedral` | 1.15 | 0.90 | the range's high point |
| *standard terrain* | — | 1.0 | 1.0 | |
| Alpine range (the lake range) | `alpineRange` (+300 m) | *0.85* | 1.0 | glacier-polished basins |
| Valley bluff (home ridge) | `front:0` | *0.8* | 1.1 | first ridge-soar should be a ride |
| Colchuck | `peak:alpine:Colchuck` | *0.7* | 1.1 | rounded dome above Shelf |
| Sea cliffs | `seaCliffs` | *0.6* | 1.2 | marine air on a clean wall |
| Rolling hills | `hills` | *0.5* | 1.2 | turf mounds |

From roughest to smoothest the spread is 3.7×.

**Seaplane lakes** (`lakeSmoothing`)
- Detection: the lakes come from the terrain's own water data, with no typed coordinates. The sources are:
  - `alpineLandableLakes`: `AlpineRange.Lakes` where `Landable` is set (Outlet, Shelf, Larch). The three lakes you only fly into stay rough.
  - `alaskaGlacierLakes`: the `AlaskaRange.Glaciers` lake ellipses (Knik, Colony).
  - `plateauLakes`: `WorldTerrain.Lakes` beside each airport (Valley, Bench, Mesa, Summit). The harbour is at sea, so it is left out.
  - `canyonLake`: the `CanyonLake` gorge, as capsules down `RiverCentreX`.
  - A test checks that every lake centre is water according to `WaterSurfaceAt`.
- Shape:
  - The core is the water plus a shore margin. Ellipses use a radial shore distance.
  - Approach corridors run off both ends of the landing axis (the long axis), plus one down an alpine lake's outlet valley (`OutletHeadingDeg`).
  - The weight fades to 0 across the blend on a smootherstep. Overlapping lakes take the max of their weights, so their effects never add.
- Numbers: `factor` 0.35 (orographic σ, rotor and lee sink), `boundaryLayerFactor` 0.7 (MIL boundary-layer σ, because water is smoother), `lengthScale` 1.3, `shoreMarginM` 300, `approachLengthM` 1,500 (past the shore), `approachHalfWidthM` 250, `blendWidthM` 600.
- Effective factor over the lakes: Outlet, Larch and Shelf 0.85 × 0.35 ≈ **0.30**. Knik and Colony are ~1.6–1.85 × 0.35 ≈ **0.56–0.65**. The plateau lakes are 0.28 (Valley, ×0.8 bluff) to ~0.5 (Bench ×1.3, Mesa ×1.5, Summit in the Frank Church ×1.45).

### 2026-10-27 — Claude · lane/claude-regional-atmosphere · regional atmosphere, calm home field
Based on split-surfaces `ed2cc7c`. Commits, oldest first:
- `61fe42d` The air differs from place to place now, and is dead calm over the home field
- `9c97ed7` The wings feel the air where each of them is, and the tests pin the new weather
- `55920df` Free flight flies in the regional air, and J shows you what it is doing
- (this log) The atmosphere lane's log

**Where it lives**
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/AtmosphereField.cs`: the field. It holds regions, the calm zone, blending, thermodynamics, the land-cover heating map, the terrain cache and sampling (`Sample(pos, t)` → `AtmosphereSample`).
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/Orography.cs`: speed-up, slope lift, lee sink, rotor, lee waves and mechanical turbulence, all from the height field.
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/WindProcesses.cs`: `SeededNoise`, `VariableWind` (airport 2) and `GustyWind` (airport 3).
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/Turbulence.cs`: extended, not duplicated. It adds a spectral (von Kármán / Dryden) mode and `TurbulenceScales` (MIL-HDBK-1797). The legacy `Light/Moderate/Severe` fields are unchanged.
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/Atmosphere.cs`: `Atmosphere.Field` (null = the old uniform atmosphere, bit for bit), plus `SampleAt`, `WindFromSample`, `SteadyWindAt`, `DensityAt`, `PressureAt` and `TryAddThermal`.
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/DataContracts/AtmosphereFieldConfig.cs` (+ loader): the config classes.
- `configs/atmosphere/regional-atmosphere.json`: the data. It is mirrored byte-for-byte to `unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/atmosphere/`, and `ConfigSync` now mirrors that folder too.
- `WorldTerrain.DefaultAirportIndex` (= 0, the Valley) is the default start airport. `SessionSettings.AirportIndex` defaults to it, and the calm zone anchors to it.
- Flight model: `Aircraft.SampleRegionalAir`, and a `WindGradient` parameter on `AeroModel.Compute`.
- Unity: `SessionSettings` (regional vs uniform), `WeatherController`, `SoaringScenery`, `BubbleField`, `LiftBubbles`, `LiftField`, `SmokeTrail`, `AnalogGauges`, `FlightAudio`, `StartMenu` (an "Air: REGIONAL / uniform" button), plus the new `UnityAtmosphereLoader` and `AtmosphereOverlay` (J).

**The calm start airport: exact definition**
| field | value | why |
|---|---|---|
| id | `home-airport-calm` | |
| anchor | `defaultStartAirport` → `WorldTerrain.Airports[WorldTerrain.DefaultAirportIndex]` = **Valley** (sim 400, 0), field elevation 304.8 m (1,000 ft) | Tied to the default start in code and config, with no typed coordinates. If the default start changes, the zone moves with it. |
| centre | the anchor's **main-runway centre** = airport + `AirportStrips[0]` offset = **(−50, 0)** | The runway, not the airport reference point, is what the pattern is flown around. |
| shape | **stadium** round the main-runway centreline (heading 0°, sim x = north–south), centreline extended **1,800 m** past each threshold → half-length **3,000 m** (x −3,050 … 2,950) | Covers final from about 1.5 km out, the base turn, and the climb-out to pattern height on either runway direction. |
| radius | **1,200 m** either side of the centreline | Holds a downwind leg flown ~1 km abeam on either side, plus a 200 m turning margin. The world is compressed, so patterns are tight here. |
| ceiling | **457.2 m (1,500 ft) above field elevation** → flat at **762.0 m MSL (2,500 ft)** | Pattern altitude is 1,000 ft AFE (304.8 m; `TugPilot.PatternAglM` 300 m); +500 ft margin for pattern-height slop and an overhead join. Measured above **field elevation (AFE)**, not local AGL, so the ceiling is one flat MSL height over the whole zone. |
| blend width | **600 m** outward from the stadium edge (fully regional 1,800 m from the centreline) | Smootherstep (C²), monotonic, with the steepest change 0.31 % of the full change per metre. |
| ceiling blend | **150 m** upward (fully regional at 912 m MSL) | |

*How the calm surface is exactly zero.*
- `CalmZone.Weight` is a product of a horizontal and a vertical smootherstep. Inside the stadium and below the ceiling it is exactly 1.0.
- At weight 1, `AtmosphereField.Sample` returns `CalmSample` directly. That sample is standard ISA at that altitude: QNH 1013.25 and deviation 0, so pressure equals `PressureAtAltitude` and density equals the ISA density to the bit. Wind, gust, σ, thermal factor, convection, slope, lee and wave are all literal zeros. It is the calm sample itself, not a blend that happens to be small.
- `Atmosphere.WindFromSample` adds the authored thermals × the sample's thermal factor (0) and the glacier wind × (1 − calm) (0).
- While a field is set, the legacy `SteadyWind`, `ActiveTurbulence`, `ActiveRidge` and `SlopeLiftEnabled` are not read. The aircraft's density comes from the field.
- Thermals: `TryAddThermal` refuses any thermal centred in the zone or its blend band, and `NearestThermalSite` moves authored ones straight out of it. In the blend band every regional quantity, including the authored thermals' strength, is multiplied by (1 − calm weight), so the edge has no step.
- Tests: `StartAirportIsExactlyCalmIsaFromTheSurfaceThroughThePattern` samples 128 points at four sim times. They are the runway centre and both thresholds at 0.5–405 m, the downwind legs, both finals, the climb-out and the cross-runway ends. Each asserts `x == 0 && y == 0 && z == 0` for the mean wind, the gust and `Atmosphere.WindAtPosition`, σ == 0, and pressure/temperature/density == ISA exactly. A 12 m/s thermal just outside the zone is active during the test. Also `CalmZoneIsTheDefaultStartAirportsAndCoversThePattern`, `CalmZoneBlendsOutSmoothlyAndMonotonically` (calm weight, wind, σ and ISA deviation monotonic across the band; steepest step bounded; beyond the band equal to a field with no calm zone) and `NoThermalStandsInTheCalmZone`.

**Regions (data, `configs/atmosphere/regional-atmosphere.json`)**
Regions are layered in list order over a global `background`. Each paints over what is below it with weight 1 inside its shape, falling to 0 across its blend band by smootherstep. The calm zone is applied last, over everything.

| region | where | air |
|---|---|---|
| background | everywhere | synoptic (menu) wind, open-country profile α 0.14 to 600 m, ISA lapse |
| coast-marine | east of the shore (box) | ISA −4, QNH +2, stable (4.5 K/km), z0 0.0002, onshore ×1.3, thermals ×0.25, slight subsidence |
| outwash-glacier | the braided plain | ISA −6, stable, smooth, weak thermals |
| high-desert | Reno | ISA +15, QNH −3, 8.5 K/km, thermals ×1.8, 3 km boundary layer |
| alaska-range | ice and rock | ISA −12, wind ×1.6, z0 0.3, orographic ×1.3, stable 5 K/km (waves) |
| alpine-range | granite | ISA −8, wind ×1.4, veer −15°, z0 0.5, orographic ×1.3 |
| frank-church | forested canyon | ISA +6, sheltered (×0.8), z0 0.8, thermals ×1.4 |
| airport2-variable | Bench (airport 2), r 1,400 m, blend 700 m, to 600 m AFE (+300 m blend) | light and variable, 5 kt |
| airport3-gusty | Mesa (airport 3), r 1,400 m, blend 700 m, to 600 m AFE (+300 m blend) | 060 at 5 kt gusting 15 |

Every region has its own ISA deviation and QNH, which give temperature, pressure, density and density altitude. Pressure uses the altimeter relation p = QNH/1013.25 · p_ISA(h), so a standard region reproduces ISA exactly. Each region also has a lapse rate (stability, which drives lee waves) and a 10 m wind with a power-law profile up to a gradient height. The wind can be in one of four modes: `synoptic` (the air mass, scaled and veered), `steady`, `variable` or `gusty`. Each region also has roughness z0, turbulence and clear-air σ, a thermal multiplier, a boundary-layer depth and a background vertical drift.

Thermals and sink come from the ground. A land-cover heating map is built from the world's own data: `WorldTerrain.SurfaceAt`, `Forest.CoverAt`, `FloatHydro.WaterSurfaceAt` and the slope aspect. It is cached on a 100 m grid. Rock, gravel and scree heat best (+0.85), then dry rough ground (+0.55), fields and pavement, then grass (+0.35). Forest becomes a weak sink (−0.15), and water (−0.7) and snow (−0.5) sink. Sun-facing (south-facing) slopes gain heat. Over hot ground the background convection is cellular, using the Lenschow & Stephens profile up to the region's boundary-layer depth. Over cold ground it is a gentle sink. The authored thermal columns still fly, scaled by the region's thermal multiplier.

**Bluff / ridge lift (owner requirement A)**
- *Speed-up.* ΔS = MaxSpeedUp · clamp(Hw / SpeedUpRelief) · e^(−agl/SpeedUpDecay). Hw is how far the ground here stands above the distance-weighted ground upwind, over a 3.2 km look along the wind. This follows Jackson & Hunt and the Taylor–Lee guidelines (ΔS ≈ 1.6 H/L ridge, 0.8 H/L escarpment), capped at 0.6 because these walls are steeper than attached flow allows. Climbing the slope at constant height above it, the wind therefore strengthens progressively to its peak at the crest.
- *Upslope.* w = V·∇h is the existing `SlopeLift`: the kinematic boundary condition carried up tilted streamlines, blocking reach ahead of the face, and the 40° separation cap. It is now driven by the sped-up wind and runs over any height field.
- *Lee.* The upwind crest is found by a soft arg-max weighted toward the nearest sample at full drop (behind an escarpment the "crest" is the rim). The lee terms are:
  - a downdraught −LeeSinkFraction·U·e^(−s/2H)·e^(−((z/H−0.6)/0.7)²);
  - a Lamb–Oseen-style rotor 1.2 H downwind and 0.45 H up, spinning at 0.7 × the crest-level wind with its top downwind, which reverses the surface flow under it.
  Downdraughts fade in between 2 and 6 m/s and rotors between 4 and 10 m/s, so a 5 kt breeze makes neither.
- Tests:
  - `WindStrengthensAndSlopeLiftGrowsTowardTheBluff`: synthetic 600 m escarpment, 15 kt, 100 m above the ground. U rises from 10.65 to 12.7 m/s and beyond, w from 0 to 8.3 m/s, both monotonic, and the crest beats the free stream by > 20 %.
  - `RealBluffHasLiftInFrontOfIt`: the Valley's first wall at x = −5,000.
  - `LeeHasSinkRotorAndWaves`: at 25 kt the lee sink is −3.9 m/s and the near-ground wind under the rotor is −2.5 m/s against a free 14.2 m/s; no reversal at 5 kt.

**Turbulence model (B)**
- Owned by the field. The spectral field in `Turbulence.cs` is a fixed set of 48 random Fourier modes, divergence-free, log-spaced over eddies from ~30 km down to ~1.6 m. The amplitudes are chosen per sample from a ladder table for the local length scale, so L can vary smoothly over the map. The spectrum is the 3-D von Kármán E(k) ∝ (ℓk)⁴/(1+(ℓk)²)^(17/6), ℓ = 1.339 L (default), or Dryden (Lk)⁴/(1+(Lk)²)³, set by `turbulence.spectrum`.
- Intensities and length scales come from the MIL-F-8785C / MIL-HDBK-1797 low-altitude model: L_w = h, L_u = h/(0.177+0.000823h)^1.2, σ_w = 0.1 W20, σ_u = σ_w/(0.177+0.000823h)^0.4. Above 1,000 ft they run to 2,500 ft (von Kármán) or 1,750 ft (Dryden) by 2,000 ft. W20 comes from the region's own profile. The result is then scaled by:
  - roughness, ln(10/0.03)/ln(10/z0);
  - boundary-layer decay above 1,000 ft;
  - the region's turbulence multiplier;
  - convective turbulence (0.6 w* (z/zi)^⅓);
  - orographic turbulence (below);
  - clear-air σ.
  These are combined in quadrature.
- The field drifts with the synoptic wind (Taylor) and slowly evolves. It is zero at the start airport.
- Tests: `SpectralTurbulenceStatisticsAreSane` over 6 seeds × 120 km.
  - von Kármán: rms 0.997, mean −0.001, integral scale 179 m for L = 150, D(2r)/D(r) = 1.62 (theory 1.62 with this cut-off).
  - Dryden: rms 0.997, integral 192 m, D(2r)/D(r) 1.97 (theory 1.93).
  - `MilHandbookScalesAreTheHandbooks`.

**Orographic (mountain) turbulence (C)**
- σ_oro = (SlopeTurbulence·|∇h|·U·e^(−agl/max(150 m, relief)) + LeeTurbulence·U_crest·e^(−s/3H)·e^(−agl/H)) × the region's orographic scale. It grows with wind speed, slope and roughness (via z0 in the MIL part), is strongest against the ground and in the rotor, and decays above the ridge tops.
- Lee waves stand when the region is stable (N² = g/T·(Γd − Γ) > 0) and the wind is over 6 m/s. They alternate sink and lift downwind of the crest above crest height, with wavelength 2πU/N × 0.35.
- Tests: `TurbulenceGrowsTowardTheTerrainAndDecaysWithHeight` gives lee σ_w of 2.29 / 2.09 / 1.73 / 0.92 / 0.48 / 0.32 m/s at 20 / 100 / 300 / 700 / 1,200 / 2,000 m, and the lee is > 1.3× open ground. Waves reach ±3 m/s in stable air and are exactly 0 in unstable air.

**Airport 2: light and variable (D)**
- `VariableWind`: a filtered random walk. The direction's turn rate is an Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process (σ 2.5°/s, correlation 40 s, hard cap 6°/s). The direction integrates the rate, and the speed wanders about 5 kt (σ 0.8 kt).
- Knots are generated every 1 s from hashed draws of (seed, index) and joined by cubic Hermite curves, so the result is seeded, deterministic, smooth (C¹ direction), free of jumps, and the same at a given sim time whatever the frame rate.
- Test: over an hour the direction visits all 36 10° sectors, the mean speed is 4.79 kt and the maximum turn rate is 6.0°/s.

**Airport 3: 5 kt gusting 15 (E)**
- `GustyWind`: a steady 060 lull with discrete 1 − cos gusts (the MIL-F-8785C discrete-gust / CS-25.341 shape). Gaps are exponential (mean 18 s), durations uniform 4–10 s, and amplitudes uniform from 75–100 % of (peak − lull).
- The lull (3.6 kt) is solved from those statistics so that the long-run mean is the METAR's 5 kt.
- Gusts sweep across the airport with the wind (Taylor delay), so they reach a downwind point, or wing, later.
- Test: mean 5.03 kt, peak 14.97 kt, 142 gusts an hour, direction steady to ±0.00° (turbulence off to isolate the gust train; it is tested separately), and the steepest onset 4.3 m/s² (a step would read about 20 m/s²).

**Flight model (4)**
- When a field is set, each step samples the air at the CG, both wing tips and the tail mean (4 samples).
- `AeroModel` gets the wind gradient across the airframe (per metre along body x and y), so every strip sees the wind where it actually is. A spanwise gradient therefore rolls and yaws the aircraft.
- `ALiftedWingRollsTheAircraft`: a thermal under the right wing gives −5.6°/s of roll in 0.4 s against −0.0°/s with CG-only sampling.
- Deterministic and frame-rate independent:
  - The sample is a pure function of (position, sim time).
  - The sim runs fixed 200 Hz steps whatever the render rate; 20 fps vs 100 fps chunks are bit-identical.
  - 200 Hz vs 400 Hz physics in turbulence differ by 0.03 m / 0.01° after 6 s.
  - The wind is held through RK4 sub-steps exactly as the uniform wind always was.
- Cost: ~10.7 µs a sample including cache fills on a flight across the bluff (`SamplingIsCheap`; ~7 µs warm), so ~45 µs per 200 Hz step. A 20 m tiled terrain-height cache took it down from 75 µs.

**Uniform override (1)**
- `Atmosphere.Field = null` is the old atmosphere, unchanged: every existing test passes untouched.
- Free flight uses the field. The menu wind, temperature, turbulence and thermals become the air mass.
- Challenges, contests, practice, lessons and events stay uniform. This covers the headwind and crosswind challenges' contest wind, and `PracticeScenario.Spawn` clears the field itself.
- In flight, K / [ / ] (one wind for the map) drop to the uniform air.
- A one-region global config is also a uniform atmosphere.

**Realism vs variance: the compression (F)**
The map is ~16 × 27 km. Real air over that would be practically one air mass. The physics keeps its real form, and the scales of the things that make places differ are squeezed so that several kinds of air coexist:
- Air masses ~10× closer together than in nature. Regions are 1.4–3.5 km across with 0.6–1.2 km blends, and differences that need hundreds of km in the real world sit a few km apart: +15 °C desert beside −12 °C ice, ±3 hPa QNH.
- The pressure differences are not allowed to drive winds. A real 6 hPa over 10 km would be a hurricane-force gradient wind.
- Convective cells 700 m apart (real ≈ 1.5 z_i ≈ 3 km).
- Lee wavelength ×0.35 (real 5–15 km → ~2 km at 20 kt).
- Speed-up relief and decay of 400 m / 300 m, matched to this world's walls rather than to real hill lengths.
- Lee rotor and sink are scaled by the terrain's own drop H, so they are not compressed.
- Airport micro-climates are 1.4 km radius, capped at 600 m AFE.
- Turbulence length scales are not compressed: they matter to the airframe, not to the map.

Tunable knobs, all in the config:
- `synoptic.*` (overwritten by the menu in free flight);
- `calmZone.{anchor, radiusM, extendBeyondThresholdsM, ceilingAfeM, blendWidthM, ceilingBlendM}`;
- per region: shape, anchor, size, blend, ceiling, `isaDeviationC`, `qnhOffsetHpa`, `lapseRateCPerKm`, `roughnessM`, `turbulenceScale`, `clearAirSigmaMs`, `orographicScale`, `thermalScale`, `boundaryLayerM`, `backgroundVerticalMs`;
- `wind.{mode, speedScale, veerDeg, fromDeg, speedKt, gustPeakKt, meanGustIntervalS, min/maxGustDurationS, speedJitterKt, turnRateSigmaDegS, turnRateMaxDegS, correlationS, seed, profileExponent, gradientHeightM}`;
- `turbulence.{spectrum, modes, seed, scale, minLengthM, maxLengthM}`;
- `orography.{maxSpeedUp, speedUpReliefM, speedUpDecayM, leeSinkFraction, rotorStrength, rotorDistanceDrops, leeMin/FullWindMs, rotorMin/FullWindMs, minLeeDropM, slopeTurbulence, leeTurbulence, waveLengthCompression, waveAmplitude, minWaveWindMs}`;
- `convection.{backgroundUpdraftMs, cellSizeM, gridM}`.

**Debug visualisation (5)**
`AtmosphereOverlay`, toggled with J (`SessionSettings.ShowAtmosphereMap`), draws:
- each region's outline on the ground in its own colour, with its blend edge dimmer;
- the calm zone's stadium and blend edge, and its ceiling in the air with posts;
- wind arrows on an 11 × 11 grid round the aircraft at its height (a green or red tick for rising or sinking air);
- the lift/sink map 300 m above the ground (green or red crosses);
- the thermal columns;
- a readout of the local air: region, wind, w, σ and L, OAT and ISA deviation, QNH, density altitude, and the slope, lee, wave and convective parts.

**Test results**
- Baseline before any change: **708 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (711).
- After: **729 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (732; the 3 skips are the same pre-existing ones). That is the 708 old tests unchanged, plus 19 new tests and 2 opt-in probes.
- New: `RegionalAtmosphereTests`, 19 tests (all pass). `RegionalAtmosphereProbe` is opt-in diagnostics (`REGATM_PROBE=1|2`) and passes trivially otherwise.

- Ty was right. "The game is seemingly launching two instances of the airplane at once and one is crashing while I'm flying the other one." There was not one other aeroplane, there were fifteen, and the crashing sounds were theirs. `SceneBootstrap.cs:88` attaches `CombatController` unconditionally, so every FLY carries six aerobatic Cassutts, two DC-3 singles, a four-ship of P-51s and a three-ship of DC-3s — each a full sim `Aircraft`.
- NEEDS YOU: this supersedes my earlier guess that the freeze was the low-altitude water query, and my earlier guess before that that it was the ditching fall-through. Both of those were real bugs and both are fixed, but neither was the freeze Ty reported. The water-query cost (Codex HANDOFF, still open) remains worth doing; it is the reason a wreck is so expensive.
- Measured, in .NET, deterministic, 180 s, the eight singles flown exactly as `CombatController` flies them (50 Hz): **legacy world 0 of 8 into the ground; island 2 of 8 plus one to NaN**, first at t=36.1 s. With the formations included, **5 of 15 on the island, 0 of 15 legacy**. Ty reported the Pitts freezing "after about 30 seconds".
- Why: `CombatZone` (src/FlyingGame.Core/Combat/Gunnery.cs) is a hard-coded box, X 1300..4400 by Y -100..3900, authored for the old world's gorge and waterfront city. On the island that box lies across **2,082 m of relief** (terrain 356 m to 2,438 m) and sits **1,523 m from the PHDH start, 575 m from 3U2's, 795 m from KMYL's, and directly over S4's**. The 3 km activation radius meant all fifteen were live from the moment of FLY.
- The crash itself, traced step by step: a DC-3 cruising level at 384 m AGL, terrain steady at 1,402 m, then the height field goes 1,402 → 1,496 → **1,860 m in 12 m of horizontal travel** near (3245, 880). AGL 384 → -66 in 0.18 s. The contact spring then threw it out at **594 m/s**, six times its never-exceed speed.
- What the freeze actually was, measured per 50 Hz step on this Mac (far faster than the phone): one AI aeroplane **flying at 400 m AGL costs 521 us; the same one wrecked on the ground costs 1,927 us** — 96 ms of every second, each, because every contact point asks the ground AND the unindexed water for a height at every one of RK4's four stages. `CombatController` never stopped stepping a dead drone. Five wrecks is 480 ms of every second on a Mac.
- Fixes, all on my side of the line (the spec says so in each zone: *"Claude implements explicit HOT-entry targets/drones spawn; COLD never spawns"*, and of the orbit, *"owner: Claude opponent/AI logic"*). **No terrain, geometry, box or world file touched.**
  1. `IslandCombat.cs` (new, mine): reads Codex's authored `world.combat_zones` — CZ1/CZ2/CZ3 as annular sectors from the origin, CZ5 as a polygon over Tern Mesa — with each zone's floor, ceiling, status, 3-drone orbit centre/height and bullseyes. Changes none of it.
  2. `CombatController`: on the island the legacy fifteen are **never created**. A zone's three drones appear on HOT entry, on that zone's own authored orbit at its own height, and are destroyed on exit, which is the authored behaviour ("3 drones per zone; HOT 3D entry only"). The authored bullseyes replace the legacy ones on entry. The legacy world is untouched.
  3. `CombatController`: a wreck is no longer flown. It tumbles in while it still has air beneath it and is then left where it lies.
  4. `DronePilot`: the floor was gated on `Aerobatic`, so **the DC-3s and both formations had no terrain protection at all**. Every temperament now has it, measured against the ground AHEAD (30 s of flight, 8 samples) rather than underneath — 15 s was not enough, because out-climbing 364 m at the 10-degree pitch limit takes 2,100 m. And when the rise cannot be out-climbed it now turns toward the lower ground instead of only pulling.
  5. `LandingGear.cs:432`: `Math.Sign` **throws** on NaN and is reached four times a step. That throw left `FlightSimDriver.Update` without its `ApplyStateToTransform` — the frozen aeroplane with dead controls from this morning's bug. A sign cannot be the thing that stops the game; it is now finite-guarded.
- Result, same measurement: **0 of 8 into the ground on the island, 0 of 8 legacy**. Each authored zone's three drones hold their own orbit for 180 s and stay within 4 km of the authored centre. **No contact physics was changed to get this** — the drones simply stop flying at mountains.
- NEEDS YOU — the cliff bug, found, measured, and DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED. Flying into a cliff is modelled wrongly for every aeroplane including the player's: the terrain's contact normal is always straight up, so an aeroplane going horizontally into rock at 74 m/s has a closing speed of nearly **zero**. No impact registers, **nothing breaks**, and the only force available launches it skyward at 594 m/s. I tried three fixes and **reverted all three**: a 1 m penetration clamp on the spring, a 20 g saturation on the total contact force, and a real surface normal from the terrain gradient past 45 degrees of slope. Together they reached 210 m/s and did break the airframe, but the saturation drove `MazeTowTests.TheTowNeverFliesTheSlitHoweverTheRopeIsHeld` to NaN — a tug on the waterfall-tunnel bore floor legitimately asks for more than twenty g, and that test was green before — and my own cliff test turned out order-dependent, because `WorldSolids` and `WorldDecks` are global registries another class can leave populated. A change to every aeroplane's ground contact is not something to ship on the strength of one edge case, so `AirframeContact.cs` and `Aircraft.cs` are back at their committed state. This wants a swept contact test, not a deeper penalty force, and it is its own piece of work. The numbers above are the whole brief for it.
- 14 new tests in `tools/FlightTests/IslandCombatTests.cs`, all passing. `IslandDroneProbe.cs` (non-asserting) holds the before/after tables, the per-step cost measurements and the step-by-step trace of a drone flying into the cliff.
- My own mistake, for the record: `IslandCombatTests` and `IslandDroneProbe` were missing `[Collection("WorldTerrainActive")]`, which 51 other classes carry and `IslandStartTests` already had. `WorldTerrain.Active` is global static state and my new tests hold the island world live for 10-13 seconds at a stretch, so three unrelated classes (`TipWheelTests` x2, `SoaringTests.ThermalCoreOutclimbsGliderSink`) failed in the full suite while passing in isolation. Fixed by adding the attribute, not by changing them.
[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: the height field rises 364 m in 12 m horizontally near sim (3245, 880) — 1,402 m to 1,860 m. An aeroplane in level flight at 384 m AGL is 66 m inside rock 0.18 s later. If that is an authored cliff face, it is working as intended and only my contact model had to change; if it is an artefact of the bounded query near a mesa rim or canyon wall, it is worth a look. Your continuity sweep reported only two cells over 200 m, both on the Drake north cliff at (-3875, -4800) and (-3875, -5040), so this one is not in that list.
[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: `CombatZone` in src/FlyingGame.Core/Combat/Gunnery.cs still carries the legacy box, ground targets and clouds, and `WorldBuilder.cs:904` still draws that box's walls and bullseyes on the island. I have left all of it alone and bound only the AI and the scoring to your authored zones. The legacy box's visuals on the island are yours to retire.

This project is pinned to 6000.3.23f1 and the patched release for that stream, 6000.3.27f2, is live now — a patch-level bump inside 6.3 LTS. It needs a new Editor from the Hub and a one-line change to tools/deploy/deploy.sh:64, which hardcodes 6000.3.23f1. I have changed neither. ### 2026-10-31 — Claude · seats and view points, loading and weight & balance, and the structural band · lane/claude-island-nav
Three features Ty asked for in one turn. All three are built, tested in .NET, and NOT yet through a Unity build at the time of writing (a deploy was running in codex-island-world and the rule is never two at once).

**1. Wings pop and groan above 1.5 x limit, break at 2.0 x limit.**
- There were two thresholds; there are now THREE, because the middle one was what Ty was asking for. `LimitPosG` stays the rated load — an exceedance the syllabus grades (Standards.GLimit) but silent. `GroanPosG` = 1.5 x limit is where it starts to complain. `UltimatePosG` = 2.0 x limit is where the wings go. `Limits.UltimateFactor` default moved 1.5 -> 2.0 and `Limits.GroanFactor` = 1.5 is new; no config sets either, so this is global and deliberate. Note this is now MORE forgiving than the FAR 23/25 certification basis, which puts real ultimate at 1.5 x limit — Ty's call, and the reason is a game needs a band of warning to react in.
- The POP is new. `StructuralState.Pops` / `OnPop`, rate `1.5 + 10.5 x severity` per second, so a slow tick just past the threshold rising to a rattle at ultimate. The SIM decides when, deterministically, so a test can count them; the BRIDGE varies the timbre, so nothing random lives in the physics. New `FxKind.StructurePop` voice in OneShotSynth: a 1 ms tick plus a high-Q metallic ring whose pitch rises with severity, 0.13 s, distinct from the woody WingFailure snap.
- Hoisted the per-step structural update onto `StructuralState.Step(n, dt)` rather than adding a test-only seam to Aircraft. That removed duplication and is the reason the thresholds can be tested without one.
- 16 tests, including one end-to-end with NO seam: an aeroplane given an absurd 0.2 g limit really loses its wings in level flight and the sim keeps flying.

**2. Loading on the launch page, and weight and CG in flight.**
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/AircraftLoad.cs` (new, mine): mass, CG and full inertia from the load, parallel-axis on all four terms including the Ixz product (the tensor carries -Ixz off-diagonal per RigidBody6DOF, so a point mass adds +m.dx.dz).
- THE INVARIANT, which is what makes this safe to add to a tuned fleet: the empty airframe's mass, CG and inertia are solved BACKWARDS from the authored `mass` block and the stations' `referenceKg`, so the reference loading reproduces the authored mass properties EXACTLY. Eight aeroplanes asserted bit-identical; every authored block asserted to reproduce its own aeroplane. An aeroplane with no loading block has no stations and is untouched.
- Fuel burns in flight: `propulsion.bsfcKgPerWs`, default a piston's 0.45 lb/hp/hr, which puts a 160 hp four at 75 % cruise on 34 L/hr — the book. Tanks drain in `burnOrder` with a group draining together so a selector on BOTH does not shift the CG sideways. NEEDS YOU: for a JET (`propDiameterM <= 0`) `maxPowerW` is thrust in newtons, so the same coefficient reads as kg per newton-second; the 737 will burn nonsense until a turbine number is authored for it.
- Launch page: a new LOADING chapter. Steppers per station, full fuel / tabs / as-authored, live weight, CG, a drawn envelope bar and the placards. Cached per type, because OnGUI runs every frame and `LoadFromStreamingAssets` reads the file and parses the JSON on every call — and it throws rather than returning null, which out of OnGUI would take the whole menu with it.
- Every spawn applies the saved loadout through `FlightSimDriver.Sim`'s setter, so Spawn's six branches, ResetFlight and a challenge's AdoptSim all get it without each remembering.
- Authored the C172's stations from that aeroplane's own geometry (real W&B arms off the authored CG). Deliberately NO `maxGrossMassKg` or CG limits: this airframe's authored `massKg` implies an 877 kg empty weight, heavier than the real type's, so a real 1111 kg placard would make ordinary loads illegal. Retuning `massKg` would move every book speed in the suite, so the placards wait for authored W&B rather than being invented. Same advice went to Codex for the rest of the fleet.
- MY BUG, worth recording: I had the CG envelope backwards — the body frame is +x FORWARD, so the aft limit is the SMALLER number — and my test encoded the same misunderstanding, so it passed. Found it while placing real stations against real geometry. Now pinned by `LoadingTheBackMovesTheCgTowardTheAftLimitNotTheForwardOne`, which asserts the direction on its own.
- Damage owns the mass tensor once anything breaks off or the wings go (`_massOwnedByDamage`), or the next fuel-burn step would quietly hand a wingless aeroplane its wings' inertia back. A test caught this for real: my first flying test flew uncontrolled for ten minutes, crashed, and came back 250 kg light.
- 25 tests.

**3. Looking around from each seat.**
- `src/FlyingGame.Core/CockpitLook.cs` (new, mine): the neck, not a gimbal — 160 degrees of yaw (over the shoulder with the straps on), 75 up, 60 down, clamped on every path including a non-finite input. Three ways in: a HELD hat pans, a TAPPED hat snaps to a named direction, and GOGGLES follow the device's attitude measured only as the CHANGE since it was zeroed, so the pilot can hold the device however he is sitting. That last choice is also why all 15 tests run with no device, including the straight-up gimbal case that would otherwise produce a NaN.
- `CockpitView.cs` (new): the wiring. The hat was ALREADY there — `JoystickInput.HatButton0` has reported up/right/down/left since the joystick work and no action is bound to those four buttons, so the hat now does what a hat does. A tap under 0.22 s snaps; tapping the way you are already looking goes over your shoulder. A pad on glass does the same for a device with no stick. Goggles use `Input.gyro` (ProjectSettings `activeInputHandler: 0`, old input handler — deliberately no InputSystem.AttitudeSensor branch, which would be dead code today and a compile error the day someone switches the handler without the package).
- `ChaseCamera.View.Cockpit` now sits at the SEAT's authored eye point and applies the look on top. The pad is drawn under the toolbar on the LEFT, not along the bottom where the throttle strip and the stick are, and it registers its own touch-blocking rect so a thumb panning the view is not also flying the aeroplane.
- Options: goggles (only offered where the device can report its attitude) and whether the look springs back when you let go.
- 15 tests.

[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: the seat schema is in `AircraftConfig.cs` and both features are built against it. A seat's VIEW POINT and its MASS STATION are ONE entry — `seats[].eye` and `seats[].station` — so they cannot drift apart. Frames: body reference frame, +x FORWARD, +y RIGHT, +z DOWN, the same frame as `gear[].pos` and `strips[].pos`, so a pilot's eye has a NEGATIVE z; `mass.cg` is the CG's position in that frame, not an origin (the comment on `gear[].pos` saying "vs CG" is stale — the code does `g.PosVec() - cg`). `referenceKg` is how much of a station is already inside the authored `mass.massKg`, which is what makes the reference loading exact; it must be consistent with `massKg` rather than realistic. `eye` absent means the camera estimates it, `tanks` absent means nothing burns, no `loading` block at all means nothing changes. Where a type's authored mass will not reconcile with a real placard, author the stations and leave `maxGrossMassKg` and the CG limits at 0 and say so — do not retune `massKg`.
**4. A global test hook that was corrupting the seaplanes — and the real fix, not a containment.**
- Eight float and flying-boat tests failed in the suite while every one of them passed alone. My first explanation was WRONG and I told Ty so: I said an island test was handing seaplanes island water instead of a legacy lake. The float tests never read world water at all — they set `FloatHydro.FlatWaterOverride`.
- The real cause: `FlatWaterOverride` is a `public static double?` TEST HOOK living in production physics, and SEVEN test classes set it — FloatTests, FlyingBoatTests, DisplacementTests, WheelHydroTests, WheelSkiTests, LessonRegimenTests. xUnit runs classes in parallel and each wraps its work in `finally { FlatWaterOverride = null; }`, so one class finishing cleared the water out from under another class's seaplane mid-flight. The hull floated on nothing and went NaN at rest. Adding 57 tests today did not create the collision, it changed the scheduling enough to expose it.
- First fix was CONTAINMENT and incomplete: I serialised two of the six classes into the existing `WorldTerrainActive` collection. Ty asked whether the real fix was being made. It was not, so it is now.
- The real fix is two lines, not the API change I claimed. I had said it needed changing "every FloatHydro caller"; checking showed ten read sites but that PRODUCTION NEVER WRITES THE HOOK — only tests do. So `FlatWaterOverride` is now backed by a `[ThreadStatic]` field: a test class cannot see another's override at all, so the collision CANNOT HAPPEN rather than being scheduled around. Every production thread reads the same null it read before, none of the ten call sites change, and the contact path — asked for this at every contact point at every RK4 stage — is untouched. Two new tests in `FlatWaterHookTests` prove the isolation directly, including that a thread which sets it and never cleans up cannot leave it set for anyone else.
- The collection attributes stay, for a different and honest reason: all five classes also touch `WorldTerrain.Active`, which remains a plain static BY DESIGN (Unity's world is a singleton) and which the `WorldTerrainActive` collection has been this repo's deliberate answer to across 51 classes. The override collision is cured; the world-state collision is handled by the existing convention. Those are different problems and only the first is fixed.
- Verified separately that there is no gameplay bug here: `EverySeaplaneStartsOnWaterDeepEnoughToFloat` passes for all four seaplanes on real island water, and `AnAeroplaneThatCannotFloatStopsAtTheWaterInsteadOfFallingThroughTheWorld` for two landplanes.

**The other failures I caused, all accounted for.** Three `StructuralTests` asserted the OLD 1.5x ultimate, so Ty's spec change made them wrong and I rewrote them (including a new assertion that the limit-to-groan band is silent but still an exceedance). One `GlideTableTests` failure was the new-airplane protocol: `unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/aircraft/` must match `configs/aircraft/`, and I had edited the 172 config without copying the mirror — that mirror is TRACKED and not ignored on this lane, so copying it is a normal edit and not the generated-mirror trap. And `SustainedPullPastUltimateFailsTheWings` was the new spec working: the Extra has a +/-10 g limit so its ultimate moved 15 g -> 20 g, the old 130 m/s dive peaked at 17.96 g and correctly HELD, so I hardened the dive to 165 m/s rather than weakening the assertion.

**deploy.sh: `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, installed on 2 of 2 devices.** The FIRST attempt failed, and it caught something 54 passing tests could not: `SceneBootstrap.cs(151,17): error CS1061: 'Camera' does not contain a definition for 'Cockpit'`. I wrote `cam.Cockpit = ...` having assumed `cam` was the ChaseCamera because the neighbouring line read `hud.Combat = ...`; `cam` is Unity's Camera and the ChaseCamera is `chase`. tools/FlightTests never compiles `unity/`, so a green suite said nothing about that line. A green suite is not a build.
[Claude -> Ty] NEEDS YOU: still parked from earlier today, the cliff-impact bug (logged above with its numbers, deliberately unfixed) and the 96-start device sweep. Neither is blocked on anything but time.

### 2026-10-31 — Claude · smoke on a button, the safe area, and Codex's water index merged · lane/claude-island-nav
- **Merged `lane/codex-island-world` (f8199cf) into this lane.** Ty was experiencing the low-altitude slowdown on MY build, which did not contain Codex's fix: they installed theirs on the iPad mini at 11:29 but my 13:01 build overwrote it on both devices, and my lane never had the commit. Clean merge, no conflicts. Their numbers: `WaterAt` over the island ring 1.622 us / **106 B per sample** -> 0.510 us / **0 B**; at KSNA 2.538 us / **408 B** -> 0.535 us / **0 B**. The allocation going to zero probably matters more than the speed: that was continuous GC pressure at every contact point at every RK4 stage whenever the aeroplane was near the ground.
- **Measured the slowdown before merging, and it is HEIGHT, not time** — which matters because Ty described it as "after a minute or two". One flight on the iPad: 61 fps at 415 m AGL, 60 fps at 554 m, 20 fps at 2 m, 13 fps at 7 m, with the managed heap flat at 32-37 MB throughout and no upward trend. It held 60 fps while CLIMBING, which is the opposite of a time-based fault. The island's inland fields sit at ~1,500 m, so his altimeter does not tell him he is low.
  - My own `cruise` self-test was badly designed for the question: it holds 3,000 ft MSL, which over a 1,500 m plateau is nearly ground level, so it flew itself into rising terrain at t=40 s and sat there. `AERO_ALT` now overrides the hold so a genuine high cruise can be flown. NEEDS YOU: the long high-altitude run to eliminate time properly has still not been done.
- **SMOKE is now a full-time button** (owner: "I want it as a full time button like gear or water rudders or flaps not in a menu"). Everything hard already existed in `SmokeTrail` — per-type exhaust positions, the airframe flow that carries each puff AROUND the aeroplane, the jet that throws it clear of the stack, a puff's own 10-30 s life — but only the Aero Widget ever drove it and nothing in the flight scene did. New `SmokeController` supplies the missing half: the ~30 s ring of samples the trail is rebuilt from each frame, newest-first as `SmokeTrail.Build` wants, plus the switch. The switch gates only the FLOW recorded into each sample, so turning smoke off leaves what is already in the air to drift and fade instead of vanishing. Off at every spawn; never joins one flight's path to the next.
  - The button sits in the cluster with GEAR and FLAPS in both layouts (a fourth row in the 3x2 cluster, with the aircraft keep-out shrink taught to divide by four; a sixth slot in the strip row), lit amber while the oil is on. Also an assignable joystick action, APPENDED to `JoystickInput.Actions` so no saved binding shifts.
  - MY BUG, and the build caught it, not the tests: `SmokeController` called `AirframeVisual.PropHubsBody()`. I had grepped, found `public List<Vector3> PropHubsBody()` in AirframeVisual.cs, and assumed it was on `AirframeVisual`; that file holds several classes and it is on `AirframeBuilder`, which the visual keeps private. Second time today I inferred a symbol's owner from the file it lives in. It mattered beyond the compile error: with NO prop hubs, `SmokeTrail.ExhaustsFor` falls through to its tailpipe case, so a Pitts would have trailed smoke from the TAIL blowing straight aft instead of from the stack under the cowl — silently wrong rather than broken. Added a read-only `PropHubsBody()` accessor on `AirframeVisual`; it is a getter and changes no geometry.
- **The safe area, which did not exist anywhere in this project.** Ty: "the landing page has ui behind the black camera pill on the iPhone screen move it down". `UiLayout` now exposes `SafeTop/SafeBottom/SafeLeft/SafeRight`, converting Unity's bottom-left `Screen.safeArea` into the top-left insets IMGUI actually draws with, so no caller has to remember which way up it is. The launch page is framed inside them — title, tabs, scrolling viewport, the bottom HELP/CHART/CREDITS/FLY row, and a dropdown that must not open under the home indicator. The BACKDROP still fills the whole screen; only the readable and tappable things moved. The in-flight toolbar had the identical defect (MENU and OPTIONS under the pill) so `MenuRect`, `OptionsRect`, `ToolbarSlot` and `ToolbarBottom` are cleared too, and everything hanging off `ToolbarBottom` follows.
- deploy.sh `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, installed on 2 of 2 devices at 14:57.
[Claude -> codex] HANDOFF: only `extra-300-like` authors a `smoke` block, so every other type falls back to a 4 inch pipe, 30 degrees down and a displacement estimated from power. The smoke works on all of them but the stack position and plume are not tuned per type, and that is geometry. The Pitts is the one Ty will look at first.
[Claude -> Ty] NEEDS YOU: the cliff-impact bug is now UNBLOCKED and cheap — Codex confirmed the (3245, 877-888) face is the authored Bighorn Crown lake bank, not an artefact, and shipped `WorldTerrain.GroundNormalAt` / `IsWallAt` as an ANALYTIC gradient at 0.5-1.0 us with no allocation. My objection to fixing it was that finite differences across that face were meaningless and cost four extra height queries; both objections are gone. Also still open: the 3.5 s locked frame on every FLY while the forest streamer builds its first chunks, the 96-start device sweep, and ten globally-coupled physics tests that flap run to run (two runs at identical code gave 111 and 101 failures; the 101 is the baseline set exactly).

## Log

### 2026-10-30 — the rigs mirror stops being committed (Claude)

`unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs/` was gitignored **and** tracked on this lane — `docs/models/sgs233-own-rig.json`
mirrored to `glider-2-33-like-own.json` — which is exactly the trap that shipped the Great Lakes with no runtime config.
In that state `git add` on a new mirror silently does nothing and only `git add -f` works, so a rebuilt or renamed
rig reaches the player only if somebody remembers the flag.

- `.gitignore`: `unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs/` and `rigs.meta`.
- `git rm -r --cached unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs unity/Assets/StreamingAssets/rigs.meta`. No `git add -f`
  anywhere; the files stay on disk and `ConfigSync` rewrites them on editor load, so the cockpit still animates.
- `rigs.meta` sits BESIDE the directory, not inside it, so `git rm -r --cached .../rigs` misses it. It took a second
  `git ls-files | git check-ignore --no-index --stdin -v` to notice. Mind the `--no-index`: without it `check-ignore`
  says nothing about tracked files, which is precisely the set in question.

Tracked-but-ignored on this lane is now the 62 aircraft configs and `build/cg-check.csv` only — both of which are
`lane/claude-config-mirror`'s to remove, by design. `StreamingAssetsTests` is deliberately NOT ported here: its
`NoTrackedFileIsGitignored` check would fail on those 62, and it arrives with the branch that deletes them.

**Why the rigs row lives on `config-mirror` and not here.** The destination name is not a mapping anybody should
type: a rig table declares the model it belongs to in its own `model` field, and that is what names the file the game
looks for. A fixed source→dest table would hold a second copy of something the file already says, and a rebuilt rig
that changed its model name would mirror to the old path and go on looking right. So `config-mirror`'s `Mirror` gained
a content resolver rather than a name field, and its row — `new("docs/models", "rigs", "*-rig.json", destGlob:
"*.json", nameFromContent: RigModelName)` — matches zero files on `split-surfaces` and picks this lane's rig up the
moment it merges. Verified there against this very file: it landed as `glider-2-33-like-own.json`, byte-identical,
and was swept by name when the source was removed.

**Gates.** Suite **1 failed / 719 passed / 3 skipped**. The 4-failure baseline does NOT apply to this lane: it
branched at `ed2cc7ca`, about 90 commits behind `split-surfaces`, and does not even contain
`AerobatGroundHandlingTests.cs`, so the three Pitts failures cannot occur here, and it predates the Extra tail move
that reddened the flat-spin smoke check. The one failure is
`NoseSkidTests.HardBrakingRolloutDoesNotTipOver`, which is **not recorded anywhere in this log** and cannot be mine:
this change is `.gitignore`, this log entry, and three index-only removals — no code at all, and no test on this
branch reads either file. **Ty: re-check that test when the lane is brought forward**; it may well already be fixed
by the ~90 intervening commits. Deleting the whole `rigs/` mirror and running `ConfigSync.Sync` brought
`glider-2-33-like-own.json` back byte-identical.

**Recommended merge order:** `config-mirror` → `flow-plates` → `extra-cockpit` → `sgs233-rig`. This lane is last: it branched at `ed2cc7ca` rather than `1b67dd5`, so it needs bringing forward first.

### 2026-10-29 — Claude · lane/claude-sgs233-rig · the 2-33's 30 moving parts, wired
Codex's v10 rig table now drives the model in play, read from docs/models/sgs233-own-rig.json itself (mirrored to StreamingAssets/rigs by ConfigSync) rather than copied into code. `FlyingGame.Core.ModelRig` holds the engine-free half — the piecewise map, the clamp, the run past the ends for the altimeter hands, the 360 wrap for the compass card, the trim quadrant's notches, and the two glTF-to-Unity axis conversions (a TURN and a SLIDE convert differently, because the importer's x-negation is a reflection). `AirframeRig` binds node names inside the placed model only and applies it.

Driven from the sim's ACTUAL control positions (`CurrentDeflections`), not raw input, so the surfaces, the sticks, the pedals and the handles always agree with the aeroplane being flown — and through `SetDeflections`, so a remote player's aeroplane and the widget's replay move too. Elevator and trim are negated once, in `RigInputs.FromDeflections`: the sim's elevator is positive trailing-edge down and the rig's is positive stick aft (ControlPolarityTests). Instruments: airspeed from IAS, the altimeter off MSL with both hands unbounded, a total-energy vario lagged a second, the compass off the attitude damped a quarter second (variation here is zero), sideslip straight from beta. The tow-release knob is animated in TowController — the rope letting go is an event, the knob is a 50 mm pull that springs back.

Measured in-engine (SgsRigCheck, new): 30/30 nodes bound, worst pivot 0.1 mm from the table's, every parent right, and at full travel — roll right puts the left aileron's trailing edge 93 mm down and the right's 194 mm up with the stick tops 187 mm to starboard; stick aft puts both elevator halves 158 mm up and the grips 185 mm aft; right pedal takes the rudder 264 mm to starboard with the left pedal aft and the right forward; the air brake puts the upper doors 188 mm up, the lower 146 mm down and both handles 181 mm aft. Cockpit renders confirm the dials: 45 mph on the ASI, 2,500 ft on both altimeter hands, +4 kt on the vario, and the wet compass reading 090 with 120 to the left of the lubber line and 060 to the right, which is the real reverse-sensing card.

One fault in the rig itself (NEEDS YOU): the yaw string's four axes are 21-29 deg off square with the string instead of normal to it, so it twists rather than swinging, and the wrong way. Wired as written and reported rather than patched over.

Also: the 2-33's wheel brake now comes on over the last tenth of the air-brake handle (AFM; `controls.spoiler.wheelBrakeFromSpoiler`), which the rig table had been carrying as advice. Local commit only; no push, merge or deploy.

[Claude -> Ty] NEEDS YOU — four decisions, none of them mine to settle:

**Tests: 1021, 899 passed, 119 failed, 3 skipped — 19 regressions against the 101 baseline**, down from 102. The 83 that vanished were not fixed one by one; they were two global-state leaks the merged lanes reintroduced, both found by the same method (a test that fails in the suite and passes alone):
- `WorldTerrain.Active`: GlacierLandingZoneTests and ModelRigTests installed worlds unserialised, and FOURTEEN classes reset it with a bare statement at the END of a test method, which does not run when the test throws.
- `Atmosphere.SteadyWind / ActiveTurbulence / Field`: twelve more classes, same shape. This is what put all 34 aircraft in SpawnSpeedTests into a stall after spawn while 34/34 passed in isolation.
Both now reset in `Dispose`. THE UNDERLYING PROBLEM IS NOT FIXED: the sim reads global mutable state everywhere and the only defence is each test author remembering the failure path, so any branch older than a fix reintroduces it. An assembly-level fixture that resets all of it between tests is the durable answer and is Ty's call.

**The 19 that remain:** GlacierLandingZoneTests (7) and MountainRoughnessTests (6) are legacy-world features the island replaced — "Expected PackedGlacierSnow, Actual Rough", and "unknown airport anchor 'airport:Bench'" from a regional-atmosphere that still anchors to the old world's fields. StreamingAssetsTests (2) are decision 3. GlideTableTests (1) is decision 4. SoaringTests (1) is a known flapper. LiveryPickerTests (1) and ModelRigTests (1) are small and real.

## 2026-10-31 — lane/codex-sights round 2: realistic sight mounts

Built on `1c038c0`, local lane only. Opened all three supplied reference PNGs. Replaced the two-segment bent carriers with two direct 12 mm tubes per side, terminating in visible metal sockets and a common hub behind the bars. Extra: two wingtip plates, converging tubes. Pitts: main waist collar plus **new** lower I-strut brace/collar; no sight brace existed before. Great Lakes: upper/lower rear N-post collars. All tubes clear structure; only fittings have deliberate skin/strut engagement. Strut bands are 24 mm wide × 3 mm wall, sockets 26 mm diameter, wingtip plates 40 mm diameter × 4 mm thick. Full body-metre endpoints/lengths and all angle measurements are in `docs/models/sights-PROVENANCE.md` and the spec.

Shared `sights.py` feeds both pure-Python GLB exports and all three existing Blender-builder entry points. Added `fit_sight_mounts.py` to raycast structure-only anchor witnesses and bake section-fitted collar radii into the spec. Changing the eye regenerates frame/hub/tube geometry from those fixed anchors; Great Lakes eye is untouched. All old→new frame y: Extra ±3.936790832→±3.936790832; Pitts ±1.850→±1.850; Great Lakes ±2.450→±2.450 (zero movement). C.x=E.x and C.z=E.z; frame planes remain perpendicular to Y. Lateral motion cannot shorten the fixed fore/aft eye-to-strut separation, and no forward frame shift breaking x=E.x was adopted.

**Verification:** `check_sights.py --report docs/pending/sights-renders/v2/check-sights.json`: 8/8 pass geometry, actual mesh angles/centres, measured Place invariance, surface attachments, static clearances, continuous full-aileron-travel and propeller swept-slab clearance. Maximum angle errors Extra 0.000000448°, Pitts 0.000003581°, Great Lakes 0.000001113°; vertical error zero to 9 decimals, both sides/all liveries. Static minimum gaps 8.647 / 15.900 / 15.614 mm respectively. Extra continuous sweep gap ≥15.985 mm; biplanes outside all interval-dilated swept AABBs. The tighter swept-volume proof uses ≤2° intervals plus a mathematically bounded half-interval dilation and the full 100 mm pivot uncertainty, covering all angles without sampling gaps. Checks include sockets and enclosing collar spheres; no clearance waiver. Props are conservatively tested as entire rotating slabs. Mounts block zero bar rays outside the central 20 mm hub join. Opaque visibility Extra/Pitts 606/606, Great Lakes 0/606; unresolved, not waived. `check_iac_assets.py`: 9/9 pass, original buffers/front panels/paint preserved, lowered Pitts card unchanged and visible from the exact sight eye.
